# Slides Skill

Use this skill as reference material when creating or editing presentation slide decks.

## Important Instructions

- [HARD REQUIREMENT] Content quality and storytelling: before planning the deck, read and follow [Content Quality and Narrative Rules](references/content-rules.md). Ensure the deck covers everything the user requested and forms a coherent, audience-appropriate narrative rather than a collection of disconnected facts.
- [HARD REQUIREMENT] Audience-facing copy: visible slide content must be written for the intended audience, not for the person or model producing the deck. Do not expose planning notes, timing scaffolds, talk tracks, content-selection commentary, or other internal process language unless the user explicitly requests it.

- Info density: avoid cramming low-value details onto a single slide. Prefer lower-density slides with high-value content.
  - Title slide: keep the title slide minimal and simple. Avoid cramming in too much information.
- Layout: keep things clean and simple. Avoid low-quality visuals, but also avoid excessive white space. By default, use equal left and right margins on each slide.
- [HARD REQUIREMENT] Overlap: always pay attention to programmatic overlap warnings. Do not assume that overlapping elements in diagrams are intentional, and do not ignore overlap warnings without inspecting them. You MUST fix all unintended overlap errors before delivering the slides. This is critical.
- [HARD REQUIREMENT] Font size: when a template is provided, match its font sizes. When no template or style guidance is given, you MUST use at least 50pt for deck titles, 35pt for slide titles, 24pt for mid-level text such as subheadings, callout headers, and text-box titles, and 16pt for body text.
- Text layout: when there is too much text, shorten it before shrinking the font size. Inspect visually for unexpected text wrapping. NEVER allow a title/banner text box intended for one line to wrap to two lines.
- Narrative copy must fit the chosen layout: shorten it or change layouts rather than adding density or shrinking type.
- Visual assets:
  - [HARD REQUIREMENT] DO NOT use Python to draw images; DO NOT use programmatic vector shapes for visuals; DO NOT use programmatic drawings of any sort. Use image search or image_gen tool instead!
  - [HARD REQUIREMENT] Minimize the use of diagrams. Add them only when requested or when a single diagram materially improves the clarity of complex concepts. Diagram implementation rules: use native PowerPoint shapes for simple diagrams; use Graphviz for complex relational/topological/network-like diagrams; use image_gen for highly aesthetic, illustrative, or scientific infographic diagrams (e.g. chemical structures, circuit diagrams, etc.). When using native PowerPoint shapes with connectors, create connectors (arrows/edges) before creating entity nodes, so edges appear behind nodes and never cross through node shapes or labels. If this ordering is awkward during early iteration, you may create nodes first in the initial draft, then switch to connectors-first in the revised code.
  - Before sourcing or generating visuals, be mindful of the desired aspect ratio, placement, and cropping options on the slide. For example, if you intend to place text to the left of the image containing a person, you should ask image_gen to put the person on the right side of the image.
  - By default, DO NOT reuse the same image more than once (unless it's a background).
  - Prepare visuals for both the main concept and decorative support.
- Default styling: use one composition instead of a collection of UI panels. UI-like styling typically includes card grids, pills, badges, button-like text boxes, tab or navigation patterns, repeated modular panels, dense dashboard-style layouts, and other component-library aesthetics that imply interactivity. Use stylized text boxes sparingly, favoring a flat structure on the canvas.

## Skill Folder Contents

Contents of the `slides/` skill folder:

- `container_tools/`: Standalone python scripts for slides and relevant asset manipulation.
- `references/`: Additional workflow references for specialized presentation tasks.
- `template_following_scripts/`: Helper scripts for exact source-deck/template following.
- `artifact_tool/`: API documentation and coding examples for the artifact tool library.
- `builtin_templates_support/`: Checked-in guidance, manifests, prompts, and reusable scripts for built-in templates. Each template owns its `ARTIFACT.md`; shared runners live once under `builtin_templates_support/scripts/`.
- `assets/builtin_templates/codex-grid-layout-library/`: Blob-managed static assets for the built-in Codex Grid template, including 26 rendered previews, a model-facing registry, structured content tokens, and 26 exact plain-JavaScript artifact-tool Compose reconstructions with no JSX. This directory contains no Markdown, prompts, or reusable runners.

## Container Tools

The following helper scripts are located in the `container_tools/` directory:

- `ensure_raster_image.py`: Ensure images are rasterized; convert to PNG if needed; quick usage `--input_files <img_path1> ...`.
- `render_slides.py`: Render a PowerPoint file into a folder of PNG slides using default sizing; quick usage: `<input.pptx>`. Output files are named `slide-1.png`, `slide-2.png`, ... in a directory with the same name as the input file.
- `create_montage.py`: Build a tiled montage from images in a directory (for viewing multiple image assets or rendered slides at once); quick usage: `--input_dir <imgs_dir> --output_file <montage.png>`. It supports most image formats with auto conversion under the hood.
- `slides_test.py`: Detect content overflowing the original slide canvas; usage: `<input.pptx>`.

## Codex Grid Artifact-Tool Compose Layout Reference

This skill variant does not include the Office template file. Use the distilled layout library as initial design and composition guidance when the user has not supplied a stronger template or brand system.

Before planning slides:

1. Read `builtin_templates_support/codex-grid-layout-library/ARTIFACT.md`, `assets/builtin_templates/codex-grid-layout-library/design_tokens.json`, and `assets/builtin_templates/codex-grid-layout-library/artifact-tool-compose/template-registry.json`.
2. Inspect `assets/builtin_templates/codex-grid-layout-library/assets/previews/layout-library.png`, then shortlist layouts by `templateUse`, `layoutFamily`, `slots`, `densityBudget`, and `typographyBudget`. Do not open all 26 implementation modules by default.
3. For each selected layout, inspect its generated preview and exact `assets/builtin_templates/codex-grid-layout-library/artifact-tool-compose/slide-XX.mjs` reconstruction.
4. Use the selected module's `layers(...)`, `text(...)`, `shape(...)`, `image(...)`, and `table(...)` helper calls as the implementation reference. Keep the output as plain `.mjs` and use `slide.compose(...)`; do not introduce JSX or a transpilation step.
5. Preserve the selected layout's content ownership, spacing, hierarchy, and media frames while replacing instructional sample text with the user's content. Vary silhouettes across the deck instead of repeating one pattern.

The shared `builtin_templates_support/scripts/create-presentation.mjs` runner can materialize any compatible built-in template for validation when passed that template's static asset root. It is not a request to emit every layout in the user's deck. User-provided templates, explicit brand guidance, and exact source evidence always override this default template.

## Workspace

Use the chat mode supplied by Codex. If the chat is not projectless, use the
project-backed layout.

Set:

- `SKILL_DIR=<absolute path to this skill>`
- `THREAD_ID=${CODEX_THREAD_ID:-manual-<[REDACTED]>}`
- `TASK_SLUG=<sanitized task/deck slug>`
- `TOPIC_SLUG=<sanitized final deck filename slug>`

Select the remaining paths:

| Chat | Scratch workspace | Final PPTX |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Projectless | `$PWD/work/presentations/$TASK_SLUG` | User-requested path, otherwise `$PWD/outputs/$TOPIC_SLUG.pptx` |
| Project-backed | `$SCRATCH_ROOT/codex-presentations/$THREAD_ID/$TASK_SLUG` | User-requested path, repository convention, or `<project-root>/outputs/$TOPIC_SLUG.pptx` |

For project-backed chats, use an external scratch directory supplied by the
host. If none is supplied, compute `SCRATCH_ROOT` with
`node -p "require('node:os').tmpdir()"`; do not hardcode a platform-specific
temp path. Project-backed scratch must remain outside the repository.

An explicit user destination always wins. Set `OUTPUT_DIR` to the directory
containing `FINAL_PPTX`. If a projectless final is outside `outputs/`, an
optional copy under `outputs/` may be created for app surfacing, but the
requested path remains the primary result. Do not modify Git ignore settings
to conceal scratch files.

### Common workspace layout

After selecting `WORKSPACE`, set:

- `TMP_DIR=$WORKSPACE/tmp`
- `SLIDES_DIR=$TMP_DIR/slides`
- `PREVIEW_DIR=$TMP_DIR/preview`
- `LAYOUT_DIR=$TMP_DIR/layout`
- `ASSET_DIR=$TMP_DIR/assets`
- `QA_DIR=$TMP_DIR/qa`

Use absolute paths in scripts and handoffs. Put every generated file under
`$TMP_DIR` except `FINAL_PPTX` and any additional deliverables explicitly
requested by the user. Retain `$WORKSPACE` after delivery so follow-up turns
can inspect and reuse the prior work.

Use `.txt` for every generated intermediate prose artifact in `$TMP_DIR`,
including plans, source notes, prompt records, design notes, QA ledgers, and
fallback reasons. Reserve `.md` for installed skill/reference files such as
`SKILL.md`, `references/*.md`, and templates shipped with the skill. Do not
create generated planning files such as `slide-plan.md`.

## Route the Request Before Authoring

Choose the output path first:

1. **Existing native Google Slides deck**: use the Google Drive plugin's Google
   Slides skill. Do not round-trip it through a local PPTX unless the user asks.
2. **Net-new native Google Slides deck**: build and verify a local PPTX with
   this skill, then import it as described in Google Slides-Targeted Output.
3. **PowerPoint or local deck**: build or edit the PPTX with this skill.

For every deck built with this skill, choose exactly one visual route. The first
matching route wins:

1. **User reference or template skill**: if the user supplies a reference deck,
   asks to follow an existing deck, or invokes a template skill, use only that
   file as the visual source. An existing PPTX being edited also counts as the
   reference. Do not mix in Codex Grid or another template.
2. **Explicit custom formatting**: if there is no reference and the user asks
   for a theme, brand treatment, visual style, mood, or custom formatting,
   create the deck from scratch. Do not use Codex Grid.
3. **No visual direction**: use the bundled Codex Grid Artifact.md layout
   library as the composition reference. Select and adapt layouts using the
   Codex Grid instructions above; do not run PPTX template-following mode.

User-provided references and explicit visual direction always take precedence
over Codex Grid.

## Google Slides-Targeted Output

For a net-new native Google Slides request, create and verify a local `.pptx`
with this skill first. The native Google Slides deliverable must then be
produced by the Google Drive plugin's presentation import action,
`[REDACTED]`, with
`upload_mode: "native_google_slides"`.

Do not use Computer Use, Browser Use, blank-Google-Slides creation plus Google
Slides write APIs, or another direct-to-Slides construction path for net-new
Google Slides unless the user explicitly asks for that alternate workflow. If
the Google Drive plugin is unavailable, ask the user to install
`google-drive@openai-curated`. If the plugin is available but presentation
import is missing, ask the user to reinstall or refresh the Google Drive plugin
before continuing with the native Google Slides deliverable.

The local `.pptx` creation and native import workflow above applies only to
net-new Google Slides deliverables.

## Implementation

You MUST use `@oai/artifact-tool` from JavaScript ES modules to implement the slide deck.

Read the local docs before coding:

- `artifact_tool/API_QUICK_START.md`
- `artifact_tool/api/API_DOCS.md`

Before running any generated presentation module, initialize its workspace so
Node.js can resolve the bundled `@oai/artifact-tool` package:

```bash
node "$SKILL_DIR/container_tools/setup_artifact_tool_workspace.mjs" \
  --workspace "$TMP_DIR"
```

Create the ES module source file (`.mjs`) under `$TMP_DIR` and export the final
PowerPoint deck (`.pptx`) to `$FINAL_PPTX`. The generated source must be plain
JavaScript that runs directly with `node`; do not require a transpiler or build
step.

You MUST NOT use `python-pptx` or the old Python `artifact_tool` API.

## Template Following

Use template-following mode only when a user-provided source PPTX supplies the
layout, style, or template. Read `references/template-following.md`, use
`$TMP_DIR` from the Workspace section, and set
`TEMPLATE_PPTX="<absolute path to the user-provided PPTX>"`.

Preserve the source deck's typography, palette, spacing, layout, placeholders,
footers, page markers, and brand chrome unless the user explicitly asks to
restyle. Do not use template-following mode for a deck created from scratch.

Create:

- `$TMP_DIR/template-audit.txt`
- `$TMP_DIR/template-frame-map.json`
- `$TMP_DIR/deviation-log.txt`
- `$TMP_DIR/template-starter.pptx`

Keep `$TMP_DIR/source-notes.txt` for content and asset provenance.

Inspect the complete source deck:

```bash
node "$SKILL_DIR/template_following_scripts/inspect_template_deck.mjs" \
  --workspace "$TMP_DIR" \
  --pptx "$TEMPLATE_PPTX"
```

Map each output slide to an inherited source slide and identify element-level
`editTargets`. Then validate the map and build the starter deck:

```bash
node "$SKILL_DIR/template_following_scripts/validate_template_plan.mjs" \
  --workspace "$TMP_DIR" \
  --map "$TMP_DIR/template-frame-map.json"

node "$SKILL_DIR/template_following_scripts/prepare_template_starter_deck.mjs" \
  --workspace "$TMP_DIR" \
  --pptx "$TEMPLATE_PPTX" \
  --map "$TMP_DIR/template-frame-map.json" \
  --out "$TMP_DIR/template-starter.pptx" \
  --preview-dir "$TMP_DIR/template-starter-preview" \
  --layout-dir "$TMP_DIR/template-starter-layout" \
  --contact-sheet "$TMP_DIR/template-starter-contact-sheet.png"
```

Import `template-starter.pptx` with artifact-tool and edit only inherited
slides/objects unless the validated frame map explicitly allows an insertion.
If no source slide can support requested content without a parallel rebuild,
report the blocker and the closest viable source-slide options.

## QA Reminder

Before delivery, render every final slide and inspect each slide individually
at full size. Use a contact sheet only to review deck-level flow and consistency,
not as a substitute for full-size layout QA. Fix unintended overlap, clipping,
wrapping, broken connectors, unresolved placeholders, inconsistent footers/page
markers, and chart/data
mismatches before exporting. Verify that researched claims and sourced assets
are traceable, and cite sources if research was used.

## Final Response

Return a short user-visible summary of the completed deck. Mention the sources cited or
used if research informed the deck. Do not attach scratch plans, previews,
layout JSON, or temporary assets unless the user asks for them.

## Codex App final response citations

Use the inline form `:codex-file-citation{...}` and place each citation immediately after the claim it supports.

For read-only Q&A, cite the source deck. For a successful edit or creation, cite the final delivered deck. For a no-op edit, cite the inspected source deck.

For read-only Q&A, inspect the complete relevant slide, including callouts, the exact question or prompt, chart or table titles, displayed totals or sample sizes, and source or methodology footers. State the direct answer first and cite each distinct evidence-bearing object when exact IDs are available.

Unless the user requests an in-place edit, preserve the input PPTX and export a distinct edited copy. Cite every changed slide in the final response. If no requested content is found and no output is modified, cite the inspected source deck with a plain file citation.

For creation, include exactly one standalone Markdown link to the final delivered PPTX. Do not add a file, slide, or object citation.

Use slide citations when slide numbers come from the latest rendered or inspected cited deck:

```text
:codex-file-citation{path="/abs/path/deck.pptx" artifact_kind="presentation" slide_number="3"}
```

Include `slide_id` only when artifact-tool inspection provides the exact stable `sl/...` ID and stable navigation matters:

```text
:codex-file-citation{path="/abs/path/deck.pptx" artifact_kind="presentation" slide_number="1" slide_id="sl/gs5z1kshq0xv"}
```

For a concrete chart, table, image, diagram, or callout, include `object_id` only when inspection provides the exact ID and you can add a useful label:

```text
:codex-file-citation{path="/abs/path/deck.pptx" artifact_kind="presentation" slide_number="1" slide_id="sl/gs5z1kshq0xv" object_id="ch/pz9t1r3ka8vn" label="ARR by segment chart"}
```

Do not cite internal previews, contact sheets, layout JSON, source notes, scratch files, builders, manifests, or QA outputs unless asked. If slide or object IDs are not reliable, cite the slide without object detail rather than guessing.

    1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
    2: # -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
    3: """Typed mirror of ``ir/schema.json`` -- the IR contract, in Python.
    4: 
    5: The JSON Schema is the *normative* contract (it is what the LLM's tool-use call is
    6: validated against, and it is language-neutral). This module is the *ergonomic* one: it
    7: gives the renderer real objects instead of nested dicts, and it enforces the handful of
    8: rules JSON Schema structurally cannot express:
    9: 
   10:   * every table row has exactly ``len(columns)`` cells;
   11:   * ``layout_id`` names a layout that actually exists in ``layout_manifest.json``;
   12:   * content fits the per-layout budget (delegated to :mod:`aippt.render.fit`).
   13: 
   14: Design note -- why stdlib dataclasses and not pydantic: the only hard dependency of this
   15: project is python-pptx. The schema is already enforced at the model-output boundary by
   16: ``jsonschema``; a second, subtly-different validation dialect in the middle of the
   17: pipeline would be a source of drift, not safety.
   18: 
   19: Round-trip invariant:  ``Deck.from_dict(d).to_dict() == d``  for any schema-valid ``d``
   20: that has been normalised (i.e. after string-form rich text is expanded into runs).
   21: """
   22: 
   23: from __future__ import annotations
   24: 
   25: import json
   26: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
   27: from pathlib import Path
   28: from typing import Any, ClassVar
   29: 
   30: IR_VERSION = "1.0"
   31: SCHEMA_PATH = Path(__file__).with_name("schema.json")
   32: 
   33: 
   34: class IRValidationError(ValueError):
   35:     """IR that is structurally wrong. Carries a path so the bounded-retry prompt can
   36:     tell the model *exactly* which field to fix (e.g. ``slides[3].blocks[1].rows[2]``)."""
   37: 
   38:     def __init__(self, path: str, message: str) -> None:
   39:         super().__init__(f"{path}: {message}")
   40:         self.path = path
   41:         self.message = message
   42: 
   43: 
   44: # [REDACTED]
   45: # Rich text
   46: # [REDACTED]
   47: 
   48: @dataclass
   49: class Run:
   50:     """One styled span. ``code`` is the workhorse: 111 of them in the 30-slide corpus."""
   51: 
   52:     t: str
   53:     b: bool = False
   54:     i: bool = False
   55:     code: bool = False
   56:     link: str | None = None
   57: 
   58:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
   59:         out: dict[str, Any] = {"t": self.t}
   60:         if self.b:
   61:             out["b"] = True
   62:         if self.i:
   63:             out["i"] = True
   64:         if self.code:
   65:             out["code"] = True
   66:         if self.link:
   67:             out["link"] = self.link
   68:         return out
   69: 
   70: 
   71: @dataclass
   72: class RichText:
   73:     """A run sequence. Accepts the bare-string shorthand and normalises it.
   74: 
   75:     The shorthand exists for the LLM's benefit: asking a model to emit
   76:     ``[{"t": "hello"}]`` when it means ``"hello"`` is a reliable source of malformed
   77:     output. We accept both and normalise inward, so the renderer only ever sees runs.
   78:     """
   79: 
   80:     runs: list[Run] = field(default_factory=list)
   81: 
   82:     @classmethod
   83:     def parse(cls, raw: Any, path: str) -> RichText:
   84:         if isinstance(raw, str):
   85:             return cls([Run(t=raw)])
   86:         if isinstance(raw, list):
   87:             runs = []
   88:             for i, r in enumerate(raw):
   89:                 if not isinstance(r, dict) or "t" not in r:
   90:                     raise IRValidationError(f"{path}[{i}]", "each run needs a 't' field")
   91:                 runs.append(Run(t=r["t"], b=bool(r.get("b")), i=bool(r.get("i")),
   92:                                 code=bool(r.get("code")), link=r.get("link")))
   93:             return cls(runs)
   94:         raise IRValidationError(path, f"rich text must be a string or run array, got {type(raw).__name__}")
   95: 
   96:     @property
   97:     def plain(self) -> str:
   98:         """Style-stripped text. This -- not the run count -- is what the budget measures."""
   99:         return "".join(r.t for r in self.runs)
  100: 
  101:     def to_dict(self) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
  102:         return [r.to_dict() for r in self.runs]
  103: 
  104: 
  105: # [REDACTED]
  106: # Blocks
  107: # [REDACTED]
  108: 
  109: @dataclass
  110: class Block:
  111:     """Base class. Subclasses set ``type`` and implement ``parse``/``to_dict``.
  112: 
  113:     ``text_length`` is the contract every block owes :mod:`aippt.render.fit`: the number
  114:     of *rendered characters* it will consume. fit.py sums it across a slide's blocks and
  115:     compares against the layout's content budget, without knowing what any block is.
  116:     """
  117: 
  118:     type: ClassVar[str] = ""
  119: 
  120:     def text_length(self) -> int:
  121:         raise NotImplementedError
  122: 
  123:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  124:         raise NotImplementedError
  125: 
  126: 
  127: @dataclass
  128: class Heading(Block):
  129:     type: ClassVar[str] = "heading"
  130:     text: str = ""
  131: 
  132:     def text_length(self) -> int:
  133:         return len(self.text)
  134: 
  135:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  136:         return {"type": self.type, "text": self.text}
  137: 
  138: 
  139: @dataclass
  140: class Paragraph(Block):
  141:     type: ClassVar[str] = "paragraph"
  142:     text: RichText = field(default_factory=RichText)
  143: 
  144:     def text_length(self) -> int:
  145:         return len(self.text.plain)
  146: 
  147:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  148:         return {"type": self.type, "text": self.text.to_dict()}
  149: 
  150: 
  151: @dataclass
  152: class BulletItem:
  153:     text: RichText
  154:     level: int = 0
  155: 
  156: 
  157: @dataclass
  158: class Bullets(Block):
  159:     type: ClassVar[str] = "bullets"
  160:     items: list[BulletItem] = field(default_factory=list)
  161: 
  162:     def text_length(self) -> int:
  163:         return sum(len(i.text.plain) for i in self.items)
  164: 
  165:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  166:         return {"type": self.type,
  167:                 "items": [{"text": i.text.to_dict(), "level": i.level} for i in self.items]}
  168: 
  169: 
  170: @dataclass
  171: class Callout(Block):
  172:     type: ClassVar[str] = "callout"
  173:     text: RichText = field(default_factory=RichText)
  174:     variant: str = "info"
  175:     title: str | None = None
  176: 
  177:     def text_length(self) -> int:
  178:         return len(self.text.plain) + len(self.title or "")
  179: 
  180:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  181:         out: dict[str, Any] = {"type": self.type, "variant": self.variant,
  182:                                "text": self.text.to_dict()}
  183:         if self.title:
  184:             out["title"] = self.title
  185:         return out
  186: 
  187: 
  188: @dataclass
  189: class Table(Block):
  190:     type: ClassVar[str] = "table"
  191:     columns: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
  192:     rows: list[list[RichText]] = field(default_factory=list)
  193:     caption: str | None = None
  194: 
  195:     def text_length(self) -> int:
  196:         return (sum(len(c) for c in self.columns)
  197:                 + sum(len(cell.plain) for row in self.rows for cell in row))
  198: 
  199:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  200:         out: dict[str, Any] = {
  201:             "type": self.type,
  202:             "columns": list(self.columns),
  203:             "rows": [[c.to_dict() for c in row] for row in self.rows],
  204:         }
  205:         if self.caption:
  206:             out["caption"] = self.caption
  207:         return out
  208: 
  209: 
  210: @dataclass
  211: class CodeVariant:
  212:     code: str
  213:     label: str | None = None
  214:     language: str | None = None
  215: 
  216: 
  217: @dataclass
  218: class Code(Block):
  219:     type: ClassVar[str] = "code"
  220:     variants: list[CodeVariant] = field(default_factory=list)
  221: 
  222:     def text_length(self) -> int:
  223:         # Only the widest variant is ever shown at once, so budget against that one.
  224:         return max((len(v.code) for v in self.variants), default=0)
  225: 
  226:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  227:         items = []
  228:         for v in self.variants:
  229:             d: dict[str, Any] = {"code": v.code}
  230:             if v.label:
  231:                 d["label"] = v.label
  232:             if v.language:
  233:                 d["language"] = v.language
  234:             items.append(d)
  235:         return {"type": self.type, "variants": items}
  236: 
  237: 
  238: @dataclass
  239: class ImageSource:
  240:     kind: str          # "data_uri" | "path"
  241:     value: str
  242: 
  243: 
  244: @dataclass
  245: class Image(Block):
  246:     type: ClassVar[str] = "image"
  247:     source: ImageSource = field(default_factory=lambda: ImageSource("path", ""))
  248:     alt: str = ""
  249:     caption: str | None = None
  250: 
  251:     def text_length(self) -> int:
  252:         # An image occupies area, not characters. It is budgeted by fit.py as an area
  253:         # claim on the placeholder, not a character count.
  254:         return len(self.caption or "")
  255: 
  256:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  257:         out: dict[str, Any] = {
  258:             "type": self.type,
  259:             "source": {"kind": self.source.kind, "value": self.source.value},
  260:             "alt": self.alt,
  261:         }
  262:         if self.caption:
  263:             out["caption"] = self.caption
  264:         return out
  265: 
  266: 
  267: @dataclass
  268: class Diagram(Block):
  269:     type: ClassVar[str] = "diagram"
  270:     source: str = ""
  271:     syntax: str = "mermaid"
  272:     kind: str | None = None
  273:     render_hint: str = "auto"
  274: 
  275:     def text_length(self) -> int:
  276:         return 0
  277: 
  278:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  279:         out: dict[str, Any] = {"type": self.type, "syntax": self.syntax,
  280:                                "source": self.source, "render_hint": self.render_hint}
  281:         if self.kind:
  282:             out["kind"] = self.kind
  283:         return out
  284: 
  285: 
  286: @dataclass
  287: class StepItem:
  288:     title: str
  289:     text: RichText | None = None
  290: 
  291: 
  292: @dataclass
  293: class Steps(Block):
  294:     type: ClassVar[str] = "steps"
  295:     items: list[StepItem] = field(default_factory=list)
  296: 
  297:     def text_length(self) -> int:
  298:         return sum(len(i.title) + (len(i.text.plain) if i.text else 0) for i in self.items)
  299: 
  300:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  301:         items = []
  302:         for i in self.items:
  303:             d: dict[str, Any] = {"title": i.title}
  304:             if i.text:
  305:                 d["text"] = i.text.to_dict()
  306:             items.append(d)
  307:         return {"type": self.type, "items": items}
  308: 
  309: 
  310: @dataclass
  311: class CardItem:
  312:     title: str
  313:     text: RichText | None = None
  314:     tag: str | None = None
  315: 
  316: 
  317: @dataclass
  318: class Cards(Block):
  319:     type: ClassVar[str] = "cards"
  320:     items: list[CardItem] = field(default_factory=list)
  321:     columns: int = 2
  322: 
  323:     def text_length(self) -> int:
  324:         return sum(len(i.title) + (len(i.text.plain) if i.text else 0) for i in self.items)
  325: 
  326:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  327:         items = []
  328:         for i in self.items:
  329:             d: dict[str, Any] = {"title": i.title}
  330:             if i.text:
  331:                 d["text"] = i.text.to_dict()
  332:             if i.tag:
  333:                 d["tag"] = i.tag
  334:             items.append(d)
  335:         return {"type": self.type, "columns": self.columns, "items": items}
  336: 
  337: 
  338: # [REDACTED]
  339: # Block dispatch
  340: # [REDACTED]
  341: 
  342: def _parse_block(raw: Any, path: str) -> Block:
  343:     if not isinstance(raw, dict):
  344:         raise IRValidationError(path, "block must be an object")
  345:     btype = raw.get("type")
  346: 
  347:     if btype == "heading":
  348:         return Heading(text=raw["text"])
  349: 
  350:     if btype == "paragraph":
  351:         return Paragraph(text=RichText.parse(raw["text"], f"{path}.text"))
  352: 
  353:     if btype == "bullets":
  354:         items = [BulletItem(text=RichText.parse(it["text"], f"{path}.items[{i}].text"),
  355:                             level=int(it.get("level", 0)))
  356:                  for i, it in enumerate(raw.get("items", []))]
  357:         if not items:
  358:             raise IRValidationError(path, "bullets block has no items")
  359:         return Bullets(items=items)
  360: 
  361:     if btype == "callout":
  362:         return Callout(text=RichText.parse(raw["text"], f"{path}.text"),
  363:                        variant=raw.get("variant", "info"),
  364:                        title=raw.get("title"))
  365: 
  366:     if btype == "table":
  367:         columns = list(raw.get("columns", []))
  368:         if not columns:
  369:             raise IRValidationError(path, "table has no columns")
  370:         rows: list[list[RichText]] = []
  371:         for r, row in enumerate(raw.get("rows", [])):
  372:             # The one rule JSON Schema cannot state: cell count must match column count.
  373:             # A ragged row does not raise in python-pptx -- it silently drops cells or
  374:             # throws deep in the XML layer, so we catch it here where the message is useful.
  375:             if len(row) != len(columns):
  376:                 raise IRValidationError(
  377:                     f"{path}.rows[{r}]",
  378:                     f"row has {len(row)} cells but the table declares {len(columns)} columns")
  379:             rows.append([RichText.parse(c, f"{path}.rows[{r}][{c_i}]")
  380:                          for c_i, c in enumerate(row)])
  381:         if not rows:
  382:             raise IRValidationError(path, "table has no rows")
  383:         return Table(columns=columns, rows=rows, caption=raw.get("caption"))
  384: 
  385:     if btype == "code":
  386:         variants = [CodeVariant(code=v["code"], label=v.get("label"), language=v.get("language"))
  387:                     for v in raw.get("variants", [])]
  388:         if not variants:
  389:             raise IRValidationError(path, "code block has no variants")
  390:         return Code(variants=variants)
  391: 
  392:     if btype == "image":
  393:         src = raw.get("source") or {}
  394:         if src.get("kind") not in ("data_uri", "path"):
  395:             raise IRValidationError(f"{path}.source.kind", "must be 'data_uri' or 'path'")
  396:         return Image(source=ImageSource(kind=src["kind"], value=src["value"]),
  397:                      alt=raw.get("alt", ""), caption=raw.get("caption"))
  398: 
  399:     if btype == "diagram":
  400:         return Diagram(source=raw["source"], syntax=raw.get("syntax", "mermaid"),
  401:                        kind=raw.get("kind"), render_hint=raw.get("render_hint", "auto"))
  402: 
  403:     if btype == "steps":
  404:         items = [StepItem(title=it["title"],
  405:                           text=RichText.parse(it["text"], f"{path}.items[{i}].text")
  406:                           if "text" in it else None)
  407:                  for i, it in enumerate(raw.get("items", []))]
  408:         return Steps(items=items)
  409: 
  410:     if btype == "cards":
  411:         items = [CardItem(title=it["title"],
  412:                           text=RichText.parse(it["text"], f"{path}.items[{i}].text")
  413:                           if "text" in it else None,
  414:                           tag=it.get("tag"))
  415:                  for i, it in enumerate(raw.get("items", []))]
  416:         return Cards(items=items, columns=int(raw.get("columns", 2)))
  417: 
  418:     raise IRValidationError(f"{path}.type", f"unknown block type {btype!r}")
  419: 
  420: 
  421: # [REDACTED]
  422: # Slide / Deck
  423: # [REDACTED]
  424: 
  425: @dataclass
  426: class Slide:
  427:     layout_id: str
  428:     title: str
  429:     blocks: list[Block] = field(default_factory=list)
  430:     id: str | None = None
  431:     notes: str | None = None
  432: 
  433:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  434:         out: dict[str, Any] = {"layout_id": self.layout_id, "title": self.title,
  435:                                "blocks": [b.to_dict() for b in self.blocks]}
  436:         if self.id:
  437:             out["id"] = self.id
  438:         if self.notes:
  439:             out["notes"] = self.notes
  440:         return out
  441: 
  442: 
  443: @dataclass
  444: class DeckMeta:
  445:     title: str
  446:     subtitle: str | None = None
  447:     language: str = "mixed"
  448:     source: str | None = None
  449: 
  450:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  451:         out: dict[str, Any] = {"title": self.title, "language": self.language}
  452:         if self.subtitle:
  453:             out["subtitle"] = self.subtitle
  454:         if self.source:
  455:             out["source"] = self.source
  456:         return out
  457: 
  458: 
  459: @dataclass
  460: class Deck:
  461:     deck: DeckMeta
  462:     slides: list[Slide] = field(default_factory=list)
  463:     ir_version: str = IR_VERSION
  464: 
  465:     # -- parsing [REDACTED]
  466: 
  467:     @classmethod
  468:     def from_dict(cls, raw: dict[str, Any]) -> Deck:
  469:         """Structural parse. Does NOT check ``layout_id`` or budgets -- those need the
  470:         manifest, so they live in :meth:`validate_against_manifest` and in fit.py."""
  471:         version = raw.get("ir_version")
  472:         if version != IR_VERSION:
  473:             raise IRValidationError("ir_version",
  474:                                     f"expected {IR_VERSION!r}, got {version!r}")
  475:         d = raw.get("deck") or {}
  476:         if not d.get("title"):
  477:             raise IRValidationError("deck.title", "required")
  478:         meta = DeckMeta(title=d["title"], subtitle=d.get("subtitle"),
  479:                         language=d.get("language", "mixed"), source=d.get("source"))
  480: 
  481:         slides = []
  482:         for i, s in enumerate(raw.get("slides", [])):
  483:             path = f"slides[{i}]"
  484:             if not s.get("layout_id"):
  485:                 raise IRValidationError(f"{path}.layout_id", "required")
  486:             if not s.get("title"):
  487:                 raise IRValidationError(f"{path}.title", "required")
  488:             blocks = [_parse_block(b, f"{path}.blocks[{j}]")
  489:                       for j, b in enumerate(s.get("blocks", []))]
  490:             slides.append(Slide(layout_id=s["layout_id"], title=s["title"], blocks=blocks,
  491:                                 id=s.get("id"), notes=s.get("notes")))
  492:         if not slides:
  493:             raise IRValidationError("slides", "a deck needs at least one slide")
  494:         return cls(deck=meta, slides=slides, ir_version=version)
  495: 
  496:     @classmethod
  497:     def from_json(cls, text: str) -> Deck:
  498:         return cls.from_dict(json.loads(text))
  499: 
  500:     @classmethod
  501:     def load(cls, path: Path) -> Deck:
  502:         return cls.from_json(Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
  503: 
  504:     # -- serialising [REDACTED]
  505: 
  506:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
  507:         return {"ir_version": self.ir_version,
  508:                 "deck": self.deck.to_dict(),
  509:                 "slides": [s.to_dict() for s in self.slides]}
  510: 
  511:     def to_json(self, indent: int = 2) -> str:
  512:         return json.dumps(self.to_dict(), indent=indent, ensure_ascii=False)
  513: 
  514:     def save(self, path: Path) -> None:
  515:         Path(path).write_text(self.to_json(), encoding="utf-8")
  516: 
  517:     # -- validation [REDACTED]
  518: 
  519:     def validate_against_manifest(self, manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
  520:         """Cross-check every ``layout_id`` against the real template.
  521: 
  522:         Returns a list of human-readable errors (empty == valid). Returning rather than
  523:         raising is deliberate: the AI-adapter's retry prompt wants *all* the problems at
  524:         once, not just the first one.
  525:         """
  526:         known: set[str] = set()
  527:         for master in manifest.get("masters", []):
  528:             for lay in master.get("layouts", []):
  529:                 known.add(lay["layout_id"])
  530: 
  531:         errors: list[str] = []
  532:         for i, slide in enumerate(self.slides):
  533:             if slide.layout_id not in known:
  534:                 nearest = sorted(known)[:5]
  535:                 errors.append(
  536:                     f"slides[{i}].layout_id: {slide.layout_id!r} is not a layout in this "
  537:                     f"template. Valid ids include: {', '.join(repr(k) for k in nearest)}"
  538:                 )
  539:         return errors
  540: 
  541: 
  542: def load_schema() -> dict[str, Any]:
  543:     """The normative JSON Schema, for the model's tool-use definition and for jsonschema."""
  544:     return json.loads(SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))

    4: 
    5: Deliverable C of the AI_Powered_PPT foundation. This is the *only* component that
    6: is allowed to look inside the template; everything downstream (renderer, prompts,
    7: AI author) consumes the two artifacts this script emits:
    8: 
    9:     reference/layout_manifest.json   machine contract  (renderer + validators read this)
   10:     reference/layout_catalog.md      prompt context    (pasted into the AI author prompt)
   11: 
   12: Why both: the renderer needs exact EMU geometry, the LLM needs a short readable menu
   13: of layouts with *content budgets* so it self-limits instead of overflowing boxes.
   14: python-pptx cannot autofit text (PowerPoint computes autofit at render time, not in
   15: the file), so the budget is our only pre-emptive defence against overflow.
   16: 
   17: Read-only by construction
   18: -------------------------
   19: The template is slurped into memory (``Path.read_bytes``) and handed to python-pptx
   20: as a ``BytesIO``. python-pptx therefore never holds a handle on the real file and
   21: ``Presentation.save`` is never called. The ``&`` in ``Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx``
   22: is a shell metacharacter, not a Python one -- because we never shell out, it needs no
   23: escaping here. (Quote the path when *you* type it into cmd/PowerShell.)
   24:

   45: # [REDACTED]
   46: # Constants
   47: # [REDACTED]
   48: 
   49: EMU_PER_INCH = 914400
   50: EMU_PER_PT = 12700
   51: 
   52: #: DrawingML / PresentationML namespaces, in lxml Clark notation.
   53: A = "{http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/drawingml/2006/main}"
   54: P = "{http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/presentationml/2006/main}"
   55: 
   56: #: The Vieworks brand red. We do not assume where it lives -- we go and find it.
   57: BRAND_ACCENT = "DB0032"
   58: 
   59: #: Ordered theme colour slots as they appear in ``a:clrScheme``.
   60: CLR_SLOTS = (
   61:     "dk1", "lt1", "dk2", "lt2",
   62:     "accent1", "accent2", "accent3", "accent4", "accent5", "accent6",
   63:     "hlink", "folHlink",
   64: )
   65: 
   66: #: Fallback point sizes when neither the layout, the master placeholder, nor the
   67: #: master ``p:txStyles`` declare one. Only used as a last resort; the manifest records
   68: #: ``font_pt_source`` so you can tell a real value from a guess.
   69: DEFAULT_PT = {"title": 36.0, "body": 18.0, "other": 12.0}
   70: 
   71: #: Typographic constants for the content-budget model. These are deliberately
   72: #: conservative -- a budget that is slightly too small costs a word, a budget that is
   73: #: too large costs a broken slide.
   74: LATIN_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO = 0.52   # mean glyph advance / font size, proportional Latin
   75: CJK_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO = 1.00     # Hangul/Han are full-width by definition
   76: LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO = 1.22        # single line spacing incl. leading
   77: BULLET_GAP_RATIO = 0.35         # space-before between bullets, in font-size units
   78: SAFETY = 0.90                   # trim 10% -- kerning, bold runs, and CJK/Latin mixing
   79: LATIN_CHARS_PER_WORD = 6.0      # 5 letters + 1 space
   80: 
   81: 
   82: # [REDACTED]
   83: # Small helpers
   84: # [REDACTED]
   85: 
   86: def emu_to_in(emu: int | None) -> float | None:
   87:     return None if emu is None else round(emu / EMU_PER_INCH, 4)
   88: 
   89: 
   90: def emu_to_pt(emu: int | None) -> float | None:
   91:     return None if emu is None else round(emu / EMU_PER_PT, 2)
   92: 
   93: 
   94: def aspect_label(w: int, h: int) -> str:
   95:     """Name the aspect ratio, tolerating the rounding real templates contain."""
   96:     if not w or not h:
   97:         return "unknown"
   98:     r = w / h
   99:     for label, target in (("16:9", 16 / 9), ("16:10", 16 / 10), ("4:3", 4 / 3), ("3:2", 3 / 2)):
  100:         if abs(r - target) < 0.02:
  101:             return label
  102:     return f"{r:.3f}:1"
  103: 
  104: 
  105: def _rels_of(part: Any) -> list[Any]:
  106:     """python-pptx has changed the Relationships type across versions; cope with both."""
  107:     rels = part.rels
  108:     try:
  109:         return list(rels.values())
  110:     except AttributeError:
  111:         return [rels[k] for k in rels]
  112: 
  113: 
  114: def _element_of(obj: Any) -> Any:
  115:     """Raw lxml element behind a python-pptx object (private API, hence the fallback)."""
  116:     for attr in ("_element", "element"):
  117:         el = getattr(obj, attr, None)
  118:         if el is not None:
  119:             return el
  120:     raise AttributeError(f"cannot reach the XML element of {obj!r}")
  121: 
  122: 
  123: # [REDACTED]
  124: # Theme
  125: # [REDACTED]
  126: 
  127: @dataclass
  128: class ThemeInfo:
  129:     name: str | None = None
  130:     major_font: dict[str, str | None] = field(default_factory=dict)
  131:     minor_font: dict[str, str | None] = field(default_factory=dict)
  132:     color_map: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)
  133:     brand_accent_slots: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
  134: 
  135: 
  136: def _typefaces(font_el: Any) -> dict[str, str | None]:
  137:     """latin / ea (East Asian -- the Korean face) / cs typefaces of a font scheme half."""
  138:     if font_el is None:
  139:         return {}
  140:     out: dict[str, str | None] = {}
  141:     for key in ("latin", "ea", "cs"):
  142:         el = font_el.find(f"{A}{key}")
  143:         out[key] = el.get("typeface") if el is not None else None
  144:     return out
  145: 
  146: 
  147: def _slot_color(slot_el: Any) -> str | None:
  148:     """Resolve one ``a:clrScheme`` slot to an RRGGBB string."""
  149:     if slot_el is None:
  150:         return None
  151:     srgb = slot_el.find(f"{A}srgbClr")
  152:     if srgb is not None and srgb.get("val"):
  153:         return srgb.get("val").upper()
  154:     sysclr = slot_el.find(f"{A}sysClr")
  155:     if sysclr is not None:
  156:         # sysClr carries the resolved value in @lastClr; @val is a system name (e.g. windowText)
  157:         return (sysclr.get("lastClr") or sysclr.get("val") or "").upper() or None
  158:     return None
  159: 
  160: 
  161: def read_theme(master: Any) -> ThemeInfo:
  162:     """Pull the font scheme + colour map off the theme part related to this master.
  163: 
  164:     python-pptx exposes no theme API, so we walk the OPC relationships by hand.
  165:     Returns an empty-ish ThemeInfo rather than raising if the theme is unreachable --
  166:     a missing theme must not sink the whole analysis.
  167:     """
  168:     info = ThemeInfo()
  169:     theme_el = None
  170:     for rel in _rels_of(master.part):
  171:         if str(rel.reltype).endswith("/theme"):
  172:             try:
  173:                 from lxml import etree
  174:                 theme_el = etree.fromstring(rel.target_part.blob)
  175:             except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - defensive
  176:                 theme_el = None
  177:             break
  178:     if theme_el is None:
  179:         return info
  180: 
  181:     info.name = theme_el.get("name")
  182:     scheme = theme_el.find(f"{A}themeElements")
  183:     if scheme is None:
  184:         return info
  185: 
  186:     font_scheme = scheme.find(f"{A}fontScheme")
  187:     if font_scheme is not None:
  188:         info.major_font = _typefaces(font_scheme.find(f"{A}majorFont"))
  189:         info.minor_font = _typefaces(font_scheme.find(f"{A}minorFont"))
  190: 
  191:     clr_scheme = scheme.find(f"{A}clrScheme")
  192:     if clr_scheme is not None:
  193:         for slot in CLR_SLOTS:
  194:             value = _slot_color(clr_scheme.find(f"{A}{slot}"))
  195:             if value:
  196:                 info.color_map[slot] = value
  197:                 if value == BRAND_ACCENT:
  198:                     info.brand_accent_slots.append(slot)
  199:     return info
  200: 
  201: 
  202: def find_brand_accent(master: Any, theme: ThemeInfo) -> dict[str, Any]:
  203:     """Answer the brief's question: *where does #DB0032 actually live?*
  204: 
  205:     Checks, in order of authority:
  206:       1. the theme colour scheme (the answer we want -- means the renderer can just say
  207:          ``MSO_THEME_COLOR.ACCENT_n`` and inherit the brand automatically);
  208:       2. hard-coded ``srgbClr val="DB0032"`` anywhere in the master's own XML.
  209:     """
  210:     hits = {
  211:         "theme_slots": list(theme.brand_accent_slots),
  212:         "hardcoded_in_master_xml": 0,
  213:         "resolution": "not-found",
  214:     }
  215:     try:
  216:         master_el = _element_of(master)
  217:         for el in master_el.iter(f"{A}srgbClr"):
  218:             if (el.get("val") or "").upper() == BRAND_ACCENT:
  219:                 hits["hardcoded_in_master_xml"] += 1
  220:     except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - defensive
  221:         pass
  222: 
  223:     if hits["theme_slots"]:
  224:         slots = ", ".join(hits["theme_slots"])
  225:         hits["resolution"] = (
  226:             f"theme colour slot(s): {slots} -- renderer should reference the theme slot, "
  227:             f"never the literal hex, so a future rebrand stays a one-file change"
  228:         )
  229:     elif hits["hardcoded_in_master_xml"]:
  230:         hits["resolution"] = (
  231:             f"NOT in the theme; hard-coded {hits['hardcoded_in_master_xml']}x in the master XML. "
  232:             f"Renderer must use the literal RGBColor(0xDB, 0x00, 0x32)."
  233:         )
  234:     return hits
  235: 
  236: 
  237: # [REDACTED]
  238: # Font size resolution
  239: # [REDACTED]
  240: 
  241: TITLE_TYPES = {"TITLE", "CENTER_TITLE", "VERTICAL_TITLE"}
  242: BODY_TYPES = {"BODY", "SUBTITLE", "OBJECT", "VERTICAL_BODY", "VERTICAL_OBJECT"}
  243: 
  244: 
  245: def _lvl1_size_pt(el: Any) -> float | None:
  246:     """First ``a:lvl1pPr/a:defRPr@sz`` under ``el``. ``sz`` is in 1/100 pt (4400 == 44pt)."""
  247:     if el is None:
  248:         return None
  249:     for lvl in el.iter(f"{A}lvl1pPr"):
  250:         rpr = lvl.find(f"{A}defRPr")
  251:         if rpr is not None and rpr.get("sz"):
  252:             try:
  253:                 return int(rpr.get("sz")) / 100.0
  254:             except ValueError:
  255:                 return None
  256:     return None
  257: 
  258: 
  259: def _txstyles_size_pt(master: Any, ph_type: str) -> float | None:
  260:     """Master-level ``p:txStyles`` default for this class of placeholder."""
  261:     try:
  262:         master_el = _element_of(master)
  263:     except AttributeError:
  264:         return None
  265:     tx = master_el.find(f"{P}txStyles")
  266:     if tx is None:
  267:         return None
  268:     if ph_type in TITLE_TYPES:
  269:         style = tx.find(f"{P}titleStyle")
  270:     elif ph_type in BODY_TYPES:
  271:         style = tx.find(f"{P}bodyStyle")
  272:     else:
  273:         style = tx.find(f"{P}otherStyle")
  274:     return _lvl1_size_pt(style)
  275: 
  276: 
  277: def resolve_font_pt(layout_ph: Any, master_ph: Any, master: Any, ph_type: str) -> tuple[float, str]:
  278:     """Effective lvl-1 font size for a placeholder, plus *where the number came from*.
  279: 
  280:     Inheritance chain, most specific first -- this mirrors what PowerPoint itself does:
  281:     layout placeholder list-style -> matching master placeholder list-style ->
  282:     master ``p:txStyles`` -> hard default.
  283: 
  284:     Returns ``(points, source)``. ``source`` is recorded in the manifest so a reader can
  285:     distinguish a real template value from our fallback guess.
  286:     """
  287:     for obj, source in ((layout_ph, "layout"), (master_ph, "master-placeholder")):
  288:         if obj is None:
  289:             continue
  290:         try:
  291:             size = _lvl1_size_pt(_element_of(obj))
  292:         except AttributeError:
  293:             size = None
  294:         if size:
  295:             return size, source
  296: 
  297:     size = _txstyles_size_pt(master, ph_type)
  298:     if size:
  299:         return size, "master-txStyles"
  300: 
  301:     if ph_type in TITLE_TYPES:
  302:         return DEFAULT_PT["title"], "default-guess"
  303:     if ph_type in BODY_TYPES:
  304:         return DEFAULT_PT["body"], "default-guess"
  305:     return DEFAULT_PT["other"], "default-guess"
  306: 
  307: 
  308: # [REDACTED]
  309: # Content budget
  310: # [REDACTED]
  311: 
  312: def content_budget(width_emu: int | None, height_emu: int | None, font_pt: float,
  313:                    ph_type: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
  314:     """How much text actually fits in this box -- the number we hand to the LLM.
  315: 
  316:     This is a *typographic estimate*, not a rendering. It exists because python-pptx
  317:     cannot autofit and PowerPoint will happily let text spill off the slide. The model
  318:     is deliberately simple and conservative:
  319: 
  320:         chars_per_line = box_width_pt / (font_pt * char_width_ratio) * SAFETY
  321:         lines          = box_height_pt / (font_pt * LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO)
  322: 
  323:     Two character budgets are reported because this corpus is bilingual: a Hangul glyph
  324:     is ~2x the advance width of a Latin one, so a box that holds 60 Latin characters
  325:     holds only ~31 Korean ones. Mixed text should be budgeted against ``cjk``.
  326: 
  327:     Titles are reported as a flat character cap (they get 1-2 lines). Body placeholders
  328:     are reported as ``max_bullets x max_words_per_bullet``, which is the shape the
  329:     prompt actually needs.
  330:     """
  331:     if not width_emu or not height_emu or not font_pt:
  332:         return {"computable": False,
  333:                 "reason": "placeholder has no resolved geometry or font size"}
  334: 
  335:     w_pt = width_emu / EMU_PER_PT
  336:     h_pt = height_emu / EMU_PER_PT
  337: 
  338:     latin_cpl = max(1, int(w_pt / (font_pt * LATIN_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO) * SAFETY))
  339:     cjk_cpl = max(1, int(w_pt / (font_pt * CJK_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO) * SAFETY))
  340:     max_lines = max(1, int(h_pt / (font_pt * LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO)))
  341: 
  342:     budget: dict[str, Any] = {
  343:         "computable": True,
  344:         "font_pt": font_pt,
  345:         "chars_per_line_latin": latin_cpl,
  346:         "chars_per_line_cjk": cjk_cpl,
  347:         "max_lines": max_lines,
  348:     }
  349: 
  350:     if ph_type in TITLE_TYPES:
  351:         # A title that wraps to 3 lines is a design failure; cap the usable lines at 2.
  352:         title_lines = min(2, max_lines)
  353:         budget["max_title_chars_latin"] = latin_cpl * title_lines
  354:         budget["max_title_chars_cjk"] = cjk_cpl * title_lines
  355:         budget["recommended_lines"] = title_lines
  356:     elif ph_type in BODY_TYPES:
  357:         # Each bullet costs its own line plus the space-before gap.
  358:         per_bullet = LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO + BULLET_GAP_RATIO
  359:         max_bullets = max(1, int(h_pt / (font_pt * per_bullet)))
  360:         budget["max_bullets"] = max_bullets
  361:         budget["max_words_per_bullet"] = max(1, int(latin_cpl / LATIN_CHARS_PER_WORD))
  362:         budget["max_chars_per_bullet_latin"] = latin_cpl
  363:         budget["max_chars_per_bullet_cjk"] = cjk_cpl
  364:         budget["note"] = "one line per bullet; a bullet that wraps consumes another slot"
  365:     return budget
  366: 
  367: 
  368: # [REDACTED]
  369: # Semantic role
  370: # [REDACTED]
  371: 
  372: #: Layout names are authored by humans and this template is Korean; match both languages.
  373: ROLE_NAME_HINTS = (
  374:     ("section-divider", ("section", "divider", "chapter", "援щ텇", "媛꾩?", "?뱀뀡")),
  375:     ("agenda", ("agenda", "contents", "toc", "紐⑹감", "李⑤?", "媛쒖슂")),
  376:     ("title", ("title slide", "?쒕ぉ", "?쒖?", "cover")),
  377:     ("closing", ("thank", "end", "closing", "媛먯궗", "留덈Т由?)),
  378:     ("quote", ("quote", "?몄슜")),
  379:     ("comparison", ("comparison", "compare", "鍮꾧탳")),
  380: )
  381: 
  382: 
  383: def suggest_role(layout_name: str, ph_types: list[str]) -> str:
  384:     """Best guess at what a layout is *for*, so the AI can pick one by intent.
  385: 
  386:     Name hints win when present (a human named it), otherwise fall back to placeholder
  387:     composition. This is a suggestion, not a contract -- a human should sanity-check the
  388:     catalog once and correct any row that is wrong.
  389:     """
  390:     lname = (layout_name or "").strip().lower()
  391:     for role, needles in ROLE_NAME_HINTS:
  392:         if any(n in lname for n in needles):
  393:             return role
  394: 
  395:     content = [t for t in ph_types if t not in ("SLIDE_NUMBER", "FOOTER", "DATE", "HEADER")]
  396:     titles = [t for t in content if t in TITLE_TYPES]
  397:     bodies = [t for t in content if t in BODY_TYPES]
  398: 
  399:     if not content:
  400:         return "blank"
  401:     if "PICTURE" in content or "BITMAP" in content:
  402:         return "picture"
  403:     if "TABLE" in content:
  404:         return "table"
  405:     if "CHART" in content:
  406:         return "chart"
  407:     if "CENTER_TITLE" in content or "SUBTITLE" in content:
  408:         return "title"
  409:     if titles and not bodies:
  410:         return "title-only"
  411:     if titles and len(bodies) >= 3:
  412:         return "multi-content"
  413:     if titles and len(bodies) == 2:
  414:         return "two-content"
  415:     if titles and len(bodies) == 1:
  416:         return "bullets"
  417:     return "custom"
  418: 
  419: 
  420: # [REDACTED]
  421: # Extraction
  422: # [REDACTED]
  423: 
  424: def _ph_type_name(ph: Any) -> str:
  425:     try:
  426:         t = ph.placeholder_format.type
  427:     except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - defensive
  428:         return "UNKNOWN"
  429:     if t is None:
  430:         return "UNKNOWN"
  431:     # PP_PLACEHOLDER enum members stringify as e.g. "BODY (2)" depending on version.
  432:     return getattr(t, "name", None) or str(t).split()[0]
  433: 
  434: 
  435: def _master_ph_for(master: Any, idx: int, ph_type: str) -> Any:
  436:     """The master placeholder a layout placeholder inherits from: match idx, then type."""
  437:     candidates = list(master.placeholders)
  438:     for ph in candidates:
  439:         if ph.placeholder_format.idx == idx:
  440:             return ph
  441:     for ph in candidates:
  442:         if _ph_type_name(ph) == ph_type:
  443:             return ph
  444:     return None
  445: 
  446: 
  447: def extract_placeholder(layout_ph: Any, master: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
  448:     """One placeholder -> its manifest row, geometry resolved and budget computed."""
  449:     fmt = layout_ph.placeholder_format
  450:     idx = fmt.idx
  451:     ph_type = _ph_type_name(layout_ph)
  452:     master_ph = _master_ph_for(master, idx, ph_type)
  453: 
  454:     # Geometry may be absent on the layout and inherited from the master. python-pptx
  455:     # surfaces that as None rather than resolving it, so resolve it ourselves.
  456:     geom: dict[str, int | None] = {}
  457:     inherited = False
  458:     for key in ("left", "top", "width", "height"):
  459:         value = getattr(layout_ph, key, None)
  460:         if value is None and master_ph is not None:
  461:             value = getattr(master_ph, key, None)
  462:             if value is not None:
  463:                 inherited = True
  464:         geom[key] = None if value is None else int(value)
  465: 
  466:     font_pt, font_src = resolve_font_pt(layout_ph, master_ph, master, ph_type)
  467: 
  468:     return {
  469:         "idx": idx,
  470:         "type": ph_type,
  471:         "name": layout_ph.name,
  472:         "geometry_inherited_from_master": inherited,
  473:         "emu": dict(geom),
  474:         "inches": {k: emu_to_in(v) for k, v in geom.items()},
  475:         "font_pt": font_pt,
  476:         "font_pt_source": font_src,
  477:         "content_budget": content_budget(geom["width"], geom["height"], font_pt, ph_type),
  478:     }
  479: 
  480: 
  481: def extract_layout(index: int, layout: Any, master: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
  482:     placeholders = [extract_placeholder(ph, master) for ph in layout.placeholders]
  483:     # A None idx would make a plain (is None, idx) key compare None < None -> TypeError.
  484:     placeholders.sort(key=lambda p: (p["idx"] is None, p["idx"] if p["idx"] is not None else -1))
  485:     ph_types = [p["type"] for p in placeholders]
  486: 
  487:     # Shapes the template itself draws (logo bars, rules). They render on every slide
  488:     # using this layout and are NOT ours to fill -- but they DO eat visual space.
  489:     decorations = [s.name for s in layout.shapes if not s.is_placeholder]
  490: 
  491:     return {
  492:         "index": index,
  493:         "layout_id": layout.name,
  494:         "name": layout.name,
  495:         "semantic_role": suggest_role(layout.name, ph_types),
  496:         "placeholder_count": len(placeholders),
  497:         "decoration_shapes": decorations,
  498:         "placeholders": placeholders,
  499:     }
  500: 
  501: 
  502: def analyze(template: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
  503:     """Open the template read-only and return the full manifest dict."""
  504:     from pptx import Presentation
  505: 
  506:     raw = template.read_bytes()
  507:     digest = hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()
  508:     # BytesIO => python-pptx never touches the file on disk. Read-only, guaranteed.
  509:     prs = Presentation(io.BytesIO(raw))
  510: 
  511:     width, height = int(prs.slide_width), int(prs.slide_height)
  512: 
  513:     masters: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
  514:     for mi, master in enumerate(prs.slide_masters):
  515:         theme = read_theme(master)
  516:         masters.append({
  517:             "index": mi,
  518:             "name": master.name,
  519:             "theme_name": theme.name,
  520:             "font_scheme": {"major": theme.major_font, "minor": theme.minor_font},
  521:             "color_map": theme.color_map,
  522:             "brand_accent": find_brand_accent(master, theme),
  523:             "layout_count": len(master.slide_layouts),
  524:             "layouts": [extract_layout(li, lay, master)
  525:                         for li, lay in enumerate(master.slide_layouts)],
  526:         })
  527: 
  528:     existing = [{"index": i, "layout": s.slide_layout.name, "shape_count": len(s.shapes)}
  529:                 for i, s in enumerate(prs.slides)]
  530: 
  531:     return {
  532:         "schema_version": "1.0",
  533:         "generated_by": "aippt.analyze_pptx_master",
  534:         "source": {
  535:             "filename": template.name,
  536:             "sha256": digest,
  537:             "bytes": len(raw),
  538:         },
  539:         "deck": {
  540:             "slide_width_emu": width,
  541:             "slide_height_emu": height,
  542:             "slide_width_in": emu_to_in(width),
  543:             "slide_height_in": emu_to_in(height),
  544:             "aspect_ratio": aspect_label(width, height),
  545:             "master_count": len(prs.slide_masters),
  546:             "layout_count_first_master": len(prs.slide_layouts),
  547:             "existing_slide_count": len(prs.slides),
  548:         },
  549:         "masters": masters,
  550:         "existing_slides": existing,
  551:         "budget_model": {
  552:             "latin_char_width_ratio": LATIN_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO,
  553:             "cjk_char_width_ratio": CJK_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO,
  554:             "line_height_ratio": LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO,
  555:             "safety_factor": SAFETY,
  556:             "caveat": "typographic estimate, not a render; the render->PNG check is authoritative",
  557:         },
  558:     }
  559: 
  560: 
  561: # [REDACTED]
  562: # Rendering the artifacts
  563: # [REDACTED]
  564: 
  565: def render_catalog(manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
  566:     """The prompt-context artifact: everything the AI author needs, nothing it doesn't.
  567: 
  568:     Kept deliberately compact -- this text is pasted into every generation call, so every
  569:     token in it is paid for on every slide.
  570:     """
  571:     deck = manifest["deck"]
  572:     out: list[str] = []
  573:     w = out.append
  574: 
  575:     w("# Layout Catalog")
  576:     w("")
  577:     w(f"Generated from `{manifest['source']['filename']}` "
  578:       f"(sha256 `{manifest['source']['sha256'][:12]}...`) by `analyze_pptx_master.py`.")
  579:     w("**Do not hand-edit** -- re-run the analyzer instead.")
  580:     w("")
  581:     w(f"- Slide size: **{deck['slide_width_in']}in x {deck['slide_height_in']}in** "
  582:       f"({deck['aspect_ratio']}), {deck['slide_width_emu']} x {deck['slide_height_emu']} EMU")
  583:     w(f"- Masters: **{deck['master_count']}** | "
  584:       f"Layouts (first master): **{deck['layout_count_first_master']}** | "
  585:       f"Existing slides: **{deck['existing_slide_count']}**")
  586:     w("")
  587: 
  588:     for master in manifest["masters"]:
  589:         w(f"## Master {master['index']}: {master['name']}")
  590:         w("")
  591:         major = master["font_scheme"].get("major") or {}
  592:         minor = master["font_scheme"].get("minor") or {}
  593:         w(f"- Theme: `{master['theme_name']}`")
  594:         w(f"- Major font (headings): latin `{major.get('latin')}` / east-asian `{major.get('ea')}`")
  595:         w(f"- Minor font (body): latin `{minor.get('latin')}` / east-asian `{minor.get('ea')}`")
  596:         w(f"- Brand accent `#{BRAND_ACCENT}`: {master['brand_accent']['resolution']}")
  597:         if master["color_map"]:
  598:             pairs = ", ".join(f"{k}=#{v}" for k, v in master["color_map"].items())
  599:             w(f"- Colour map: {pairs}")
  600:         w("")
  601:         w("### Layouts")
  602:         w("")
  603:         w("| # | layout_id | role | placeholders |")
  604:         w("|---|-----------|------|--------------|")
  605:         for lay in master["layouts"]:
  606:             types = ", ".join(f"{p['type']}(idx {p['idx']})" for p in lay["placeholders"]) or "-none-"
  607:             w(f"| {lay['index']} | `{lay['layout_id']}` | {lay['semantic_role']} | {types} |")
  608:         w("")
  609: 
  610:         w("### Content budgets")
  611:         w("")
  612:         w("The AI author MUST respect these. `cjk` applies to Korean text, `latin` to English;")
  613:         w("for mixed text, budget against `cjk`.")
  614:         w("")
  615:         for lay in master["layouts"]:
  616:             if not lay["placeholders"]:
  617:                 continue
  618:             w(f"**`{lay['layout_id']}`** ({lay['semantic_role']})")
  619:             w("")
  620:             for ph in lay["placeholders"]:
  621:                 b = ph["content_budget"]
  622:                 head = f"- `idx {ph['idx']}` {ph['type']} -- {ph['inches']['width']}x{ph['inches']['height']}in @ {ph['font_pt']}pt"
  623:                 if not b.get("computable"):
  624:                     w(head + f" -- budget unavailable ({b.get('reason')})")
  625:                     continue
  626:                 if "max_title_chars_cjk" in b:
  627:                     w(head + f" -- max **{b['max_title_chars_latin']} latin chars** "
  628:                              f"/ **{b['max_title_chars_cjk']} korean chars**, "
  629:                              f"<= {b['recommended_lines']} lines")
  630:                 elif "max_bullets" in b:
  631:                     w(head + f" -- max **{b['max_bullets']} bullets** x "
  632:                              f"**{b['max_words_per_bullet']} words** "
  633:                              f"({b['max_chars_per_bullet_latin']} latin / "
  634:                              f"{b['max_chars_per_bullet_cjk']} korean chars per bullet)")
  635:                 else:
  636:                     w(head + f" -- {b['chars_per_line_latin']} latin chars/line, "
  637:                              f"{b['max_lines']} lines")
  638:             w("")
  639: 
  640:     w("---")
  641:     w("")
  642:     w("## Allowed `layout_id` values")
  643:     w("")
  644:     w("The IR `layout_id` field MUST be exactly one of:")
  645:     w("")
  646:     for master in manifest["masters"]:
  647:         for lay in master["layouts"]:
  648:             w(f"- `{lay['layout_id']}`  ({lay['semantic_role']})")
  649:     w("")
  650:     return "\n".join(out) + "\n"
  651: 
  652: 
  653: def print_summary(manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
  654:     deck = manifest["deck"]
  655:     print("=" * 78)
  656:     print(f"  {manifest['source']['filename']}")
  657:     print("=" * 78)
  658:     print(f"  slide size : {deck['slide_width_in']}in x {deck['slide_height_in']}in  "
  659:           f"({deck['aspect_ratio']})  [{deck['slide_width_emu']} x {deck['slide_height_emu']} EMU]")
  660:     print(f"  masters    : {deck['master_count']}")
  661:     print(f"  layouts    : {deck['layout_count_first_master']} (first master)")
  662:     print(f"  slides     : {deck['existing_slide_count']} already in the template")
  663:     print()
  664: 
  665:     for master in manifest["masters"]:
  666:         print(f"MASTER {master['index']}: {master['name']}  (theme: {master['theme_name']})")
  667:         major = master["font_scheme"].get("major") or {}
  668:         minor = master["font_scheme"].get("minor") or {}
  669:         print(f"  fonts  : major latin={major.get('latin')} ea={major.get('ea')} | "
  670:               f"minor latin={minor.get('latin')} ea={minor.get('ea')}")
  671:         print(f"  #{BRAND_ACCENT}: {master['brand_accent']['resolution']}")
  672:         if master["color_map"]:
  673:             print("  colors : " + ", ".join(f"{k}=#{v}" for k, v in master["color_map"].items()))
  674:         print()
  675:         for lay in master["layouts"]:
  676:             print(f"  [{lay['index']:>2}] {lay['layout_id']!r}  "
  677:                   f"role={lay['semantic_role']}  placeholders={lay['placeholder_count']}")
  678:             for ph in lay["placeholders"]:
  679:                 inch = ph["inches"]
  680:                 mark = " (inherited)" if ph["geometry_inherited_from_master"] else ""
  681:                 print(f"        idx={ph['idx']:<3} {ph['type']:<14} {ph['name']!r}")
  682:                 print(f"          box  : L={inch['left']} T={inch['top']} "
  683:                       f"W={inch['width']} H={inch['height']} in{mark}")
  684:                 print(f"          font : {ph['font_pt']}pt  (from {ph['font_pt_source']})")
  685:                 b = ph["content_budget"]
  686:                 if not b.get("computable"):
  687:                     print(f"          budget: n/a - {b.get('reason')}")
  688:                 elif "max_title_chars_cjk" in b:
  689:                     print(f"          budget: title <= {b['max_title_chars_latin']} latin "
  690:                           f"/ {b['max_title_chars_cjk']} korean chars")
  691:                 elif "max_bullets" in b:
  692:                     print(f"          budget: {b['max_bullets']} bullets x "
  693:                           f"{b['max_words_per_bullet']} words "
  694:                           f"({b['max_chars_per_bullet_cjk']} korean chars/bullet)")
  695:                 else:
  696:                     print(f"          budget: {b['chars_per_line_latin']} latin chars/line "
  697:                           f"x {b['max_lines']} lines")
  698:             if lay["decoration_shapes"]:
  699:                 print(f"        decoration (drawn by the template, not fillable): "
  700:                       f"{', '.join(lay['decoration_shapes'])}")
  701:             print()
  702: 
  703:     if manifest["existing_slides"]:
  704:         print("EXISTING SLIDES IN TEMPLATE")
  705:         for s in manifest["existing_slides"]:
  706:             print(f"  [{s['index']:>2}] layout={s['layout']!r} shapes={s['shape_count']}")
  707:         print()

  709: 
  710: # [REDACTED]
  711: # CLI
  712: # [REDACTED]
  713: 
  714: DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME = "Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx"
  715: 
  716: 
  717: def find_default_template(start: Path) -> Path | None:
  718:     """Look for the template beside the repo, then walk up. Keeps the no-arg case working."""
  719:     for base in (start, *start.parents):
  720:         candidate = base / DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME
  721:         if candidate.is_file():
  722:             return candidate
  723:     return None
  724: 
  725: 
  726: def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
  727:     here = Path(__file__).resolve()
  728:     repo_root = here.parents[2]          # .../src/aippt/x.py -> repo root
  729: 
  730:     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
  731:         description="Extract layouts, theme and content budgets from the PPTX master.")
  732:     parser.add_argument("--template", type=Path, default=None,
  733:                         help=f"path to the .pptx (default: nearest {DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME})")
  734:     parser.add_argument("--out-dir", type=Path, default=repo_root / "reference",
  735:                         help="where to write layout_manifest.json / layout_catalog.md")
  736:     parser.add_argument("--quiet", action="store_true", help="suppress the stdout summary")
  737:     args = parser.parse_args(argv)
  738: 
  739:     # Korean layout names and Hangul in the summary must survive a Windows console.
  740:     try:
  741:         sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
  742:     except (AttributeError, ValueError):  # pragma: no cover - very old / redirected stdout
  743:         pass
  744: 
  745:     template = args.template or find_default_template(repo_root)
  746:     if template is None:
  747:         print(f"ERROR: could not find {DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME}. Pass --template <path>.",
  748:               file=sys.stderr)
  749:         return 2
  750:     template = Path(template).expanduser().resolve()
  751:     if not template.is_file():
  752:         print(f"ERROR: template not found: {template}", file=sys.stderr)
  753:         return 2
  754: 
  755:     try:
  756:         import pptx  # noqa: F401
  757:     except ImportError:
  758:         print("ERROR: python-pptx is not installed.  pip install -r requirements.txt",
  759:               file=sys.stderr)
  760:         return 3
  761: 
  762:     try:
  763:         manifest = analyze(template)
  764:     except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - top-level CLI guard, we want the message
  765:         print(f"ERROR: failed to parse {template.name}: {exc!r}", file=sys.stderr)
  766:         return 4
  767: 
  768:     out_dir: Path = args.out_dir
  769:     out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  770: 
  771:     json_path = out_dir / "layout_manifest.json"
  772:     md_path = out_dir / "layout_catalog.md"
  773:     json_path.write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
  774:     md_path.write_text(render_catalog(manifest), encoding="utf-8")
  775: 
  776:     if not args.quiet:
  777:         print_summary(manifest)
  778:     print(f"wrote {json_path}")
  779:     print(f"wrote {md_path}")
  780:     return 0
  781: 
  782: 
  783: if __name__ == "__main__":
  784:     raise SystemExit(main())

   72: `add_picture()` silently stretches images instead of aspect-fitting them.
   73:

   75: poppler (`pdftoppm`), and Node + `@mermaid-js/mermaid-cli` (`mmdc`).

  127: |---|---|
  128: | `Slide Template.html` | 4 |

  133: slide, and each card holds exactly one `<h2>` (30 cards, 30 `<h2>` ??verified).

  136:

  138: |---|---|---|
  139: | inline `<code>` | **111** | run-level styling is mandatory, not a nicety |
  140: | `<h3>` | 28 | `heading` is a first-class block |
  141: | `<ul>` | 22 | |

  145: | base64 images | 3 | all `image/jpeg` ??**not** png |
  146: | `<pre>` | 2 | one of them is a tabbed C++/C# pair |

  149: `.alert-box` callout, PNG images, and did not mention `.tabs`, `.step-badge` (횞10),
  150: `.mini-card` (횞9) or the `sim-*` interactive panels at all. The IR follows the corpus, not
  151: the brief. See `foundation_research.md` 짠3.

  156: 
  157: | Phase | Scope | Gate to pass |
  158: |---|---|---|
  159: | **0** | Run the analyzer. Get real layout facts. | `layout_manifest.json` has real data |
  160: | **1** | `html_adapter` ??IR ??`renderer` ??`.pptx`. **No AI.** | All 30 corpus slides round-trip with a clean `ConversionReport` |
  161: | **2** | `ai_adapter`: topic + sources ??IR, structured output + bounded retry | A generated deck opens in PowerPoint, on-brand, nothing overflowing |
  162: | **3** | Render ??PNG ??deterministic geometry checks ??vision critique ??repair | The loop converges without a human |
  163: 
  164: Phase 1 has no AI in it on purpose. If a deck we *already have* cannot survive the
  165: round trip, no amount of prompt engineering will save the AI path ??and Phase 1 failures
  166: are debuggable in a way that "the model wrote something odd" never is.
