   34: ---
   35: 
   36: ## 1. Problem, goals, and the three approaches we reject
   37: 
   38: ### The problem
   39: 
   40: Vieworks R&D produces technical decks ??SDK guides, architecture proposals, test-bench
   41: designs. Today they are authored as hand-built HTML "card" decks (30 slides across three
   42: files, see 짠3). They look good in a browser, and they are not PowerPoint: they cannot be
   43: handed to a colleague to edit, they do not carry the corporate master, and they cannot be
   44: dropped into a customer-facing deck.
   45: 
   46: Meanwhile `Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx` ??the real corporate template, with the real
   47: master, the real fonts and the real brand red ??sits unused by this workflow.
   48: 
   49: ### Goals
   50: 
   51: 1. Generate `.pptx` decks that are **indistinguishable from human-authored ones built on
   52:    the corporate template** ??because they *are* built on it.
   53: 2. Output must be **fully editable**: real text in real placeholders, real shapes. A
   54:    designer must be able to open it and move things.
   55: 3. **Two front doors, one engine:** convert the existing HTML decks, *and* author new decks
   56:    from a topic + source material.
   57: 4. **Model-agnostic.** Claude, Codex and Gemini are swappable authors behind one interface.
   58: 
   59: ### Rejected: (a) headless-browser screenshot ??image slides
   60: 
   61: Render the HTML in Chrome, screenshot each card, `add_picture` it onto a blank slide.
   62: 
   63: Seductive because it is a weekend's work and the output looks *pixel-identical* to the
   64: HTML. Rejected because it produces a deck of **pictures of slides**:
   65: 
   66: - Nothing is editable. Not one word. Fixing a typo means re-rendering the source HTML ??  which most of the audience for these decks does not have and could not run.
   67: - The corporate master is bypassed entirely. The output inherits the *browser's*
   68:   typography, not the template's. It only coincidentally resembles a Vieworks deck.
   69: - No text layer: no search, no screen reader, no copy-paste, no reflow.
   70: - It hard-codes the current HTML CSS as the de facto design system, permanently. The
   71:   designer who owns the master has been written out of the loop.
   72: 
   73: This approach optimises for the demo and sacrifices the product. It is the one to be most
   74: careful about, precisely because the first screenshot looks so convincing.
   75: 
   76: ### Rejected: (b) the LLM writes DrawingML / OOXML directly
   77: 
   78: Ask the model for `<p:sp>` elements.
   79: 
   80: - OOXML is verbose, deeply namespaced, and unforgiving. A single misplaced element yields
   81:   "PowerPoint found unreadable content" ??with no indication of which of 4,000 lines is at
   82:   fault.
   83: - The failure mode is *binary and opaque*. There is no partial credit and no useful error,
   84:   so the repair loop has nothing to work with.
   85: - It burns an enormous number of tokens on syntax that a template already encodes perfectly.
   86: - It puts geometry in the hands of the component with the least reliable grasp of it.
   87:   Models are excellent at prose and structure; they are not a layout engine.
   88: 
   89: ### Rejected: (c) build from a blank `Presentation()`
   90: 
   91: Create an empty deck and restyle it to match the brand.
   92: 
   93: - `Presentation()` with no argument loads python-pptx's **stock 4:3 Office template**. Not
   94:   the Vieworks master. ?뱰
   95: - Every colour, font, size and position would have to be re-applied by hand ??a
   96:   reimplementation of the template in Python, which must then be kept in sync with a
   97:   `.pptx` that a designer will keep editing. It will drift, and the drift will be silent.
   98: - The master, the layouts and the theme are *gone*. A user who opens the deck and picks
   99:   "New Slide" gets nothing that matches.
  100: 
  101: The template is not a style guide to be imitated. It is the source of truth, and the
  102: correct move is to **open it and add slides to it**.
  103: 
  104: ### What we do instead
  105: 
  106: Open the real template. Call `add_slide()` with the template's **own** `slide_layouts`. Put
  107: text in placeholders. Let the master do what a master is for.
  108: 
  109: ---
  110: 
  111: ## 2. The pipeline
  112: 
  113: ```
  114:                           reference/layout_manifest.json
  115:                           reference/layout_catalog.md
  116:                                     ?? (generated once by analyze_pptx_master.py)
  117:                                     ??                                    ??  HTML deck ??> html_adapter ????        ?뚢??> layout_id + content budgets
  118:                                ?쒋?> [ Slide IR ] ?> renderer ?> deck.pptx
  119:   topic + sources ?> ai_adapter??    (JSON,          ??                          ??          schema-        ??                          ??          validated)     ??                          ??                   LibreOffice ?> PDF ?> PNG
  120:                           ??                         ??                          ??                         ??                          ??                geometry checks (fit.py)
  121:                           ??                         +
  122:                           ?붴????? repair ?꾟???? vision critique (30_visual_qa.md)
  123: ```
  124: 
  125: Four properties this shape buys, none of which are free:
  126: 
  127: **The IR is the contract.** Adapters and renderer are coupled only through
  128: `ir/schema.json`. A new input source (Markdown, Confluence, a Word doc) is a new adapter
  129: and nothing else changes. A new output target is a new renderer.
  130: 
  131: **The AI is confined to one box.** It writes `layout_id` + content. It cannot emit
  132: geometry because the schema has nowhere to put geometry. The invariant is enforced by the
  133: data model, not by asking the model nicely ??which is the only kind of enforcement that
  134: survives contact with a model that is having an off day.
  135: 
  136: **Determinism where determinism is possible.** Layout, geometry and styling are code.
  137: Only *content* is probabilistic. When a deck comes out wrong, the question "is this a
  138: model problem or a code problem?" has a crisp answer.
  139: 
  140: **Model-agnosticism is structural, not aspirational.** All three providers can constrain
  141: output to a JSON Schema. The schema is the neutral contract; the provider is an
  142: implementation detail behind `LLMClient`.
  143: 
  144: ---
  145: 
  146: ## 3. The Slide IR ??and how the corpus reshaped it ??
  147: The full annotated schema is `src/aippt/ir/schema.json`; the typed mirror is
  148: `src/aippt/ir/models.py`. This section explains *why* it has the shape it has.
  149: 
  150: ### The corpus is the spec
  151: 
  152: The block taxonomy was not designed in the abstract. It was **counted**, directly, out of
  153: the three existing decks:
  154: 
  155: | Construct | Occurrences | What it forced |
  156: |---|---:|---|
  157: | `<section class="card">` | **30** | one card = one slide; the whole IR hangs off this |
  158: | `<h2>` | **30** | exactly one title per card ??an invariant, not a convention |
  159: | inline `<code>` | **111** | `rich_text` runs are **mandatory** |
  160: | `<h3>` | 28 | `heading` is a first-class block |
  161: | `<ul>` | 22 | `bullets`, with levels |
  162: | `<table>` | 11 | `table` ??and this is the block most likely to overflow |
  163: | `.step-badge` | 10 | `steps` |
  164: | `.mini-card` | 9 | `cards` (in `.grid-2` / `.grid-3`) |
  165: | `.tag` | 8 | a field on `cards` |
  166: | `<svg>` | 7 | hand-drawn diagrams ??the mermaid path will **not** catch these |
  167: | `.alert-box` | 4 | `callout`, variant `info` |
  168: | base64 `<img>` | **3** | all `image/jpeg` |
  169: | `.mermaid` | 3 | `diagram` |
  170: | `.warn-box` | 2 | `callout`, variant `warn` |
  171: | `<pre>` | 2 | `code` |
  172: | `.ok-box` | 1 | `callout`, variant `ok` |
  173: | `.tabs` | 1 | `code.variants[]` ??a tabbed C++/C# pair |
  174: 
  175: ### Four things the corpus contradicted
  176: 
  177: The brief that scoped this work described the taxonomy from memory. Reading the actual
  178: files corrected it in four ways, each of which changed the schema:
  179: 
  180: 1. **Callouts have three variants, not one.** `.alert-box`, `.warn-box`, `.ok-box`. A
  181:    single `callout` block with a `variant` discriminator, since they differ only in accent
  182:    colour.
  183: 2. **The embedded images are JPEG, not PNG.** Harmless for `add_picture` (it sniffs the
  184:    header), but the data-URI parser must not hard-code `image/png`.
  185: 3. **`.tabs` exists and nothing in the brief mentioned it.** Slide Template.html slide 4 is
  186:    a tabbed C++/C# code pair. PowerPoint has no tab control, so `code` carries a
  187:    `variants[]` array and the *renderer* decides how to realise it ??side-by-side, or two
  188:    slides. Content decision, not a styling one, hence it lives in the IR.
  189: 4. **Inline `<code>` is the dominant inline mark ??111 of them.** This is the finding with
  190:    the largest downstream consequence. It rules out the tempting simplification of
  191:    modelling text as plain strings: a deck of API documentation that renders
  192:    `VEO_Focus_Initialize()` as ordinary body prose looks broken to exactly the audience
  193:    these decks are for. Hence `rich_text` with run-level styling.
  194: 
  195: `rich_text` accepts **either** a bare string **or** a run array. That is a concession to
  196: the LLM, and a deliberate one: asking a model to emit `[{"t": "hello"}]` when it means
  197: `"hello"` is a reliable generator of malformed output. `models.py` normalises the string
  198: form inward, so the renderer only ever sees runs.
  199: 
  200: ### What the IR deliberately refuses to model
  201: 
  202: - **`sim-*` interactive panels** (sliders, control panels ??3D Logic deck). There is no
  203:   static representation of an interactive simulator. The adapter must emit a **lossy
  204:   warning**, not silently drop it. A slide whose entire point was the simulator becoming a
  205:   blank slide, quietly, is the failure mode we are guarding against.
  206: - **`<svg>` hand-drawn diagrams** (7 of them). Not mermaid, so `diagrams.py` cannot parse
  207:   them. They must be rasterised or re-authored. Also a lossy warning.
  208: - **Geometry, colour, fonts, sizes.** There is nowhere in the schema to put them. That is
  209:   the invariant, expressed as a data structure.
  210: 
  211: ---
  212: 
  213: ## 4. Master and layout handling
  214: 
  215: ### ??The actual inventory ??NOT AVAILABLE
  216: 
  217: **This is the hole in this document.** The PoC never ran (see the top of this file), so the
  218: following are all unknown:
  219: 
  220: - slide size (EMU / inches / aspect ratio) ????- number of masters ????- number of layouts, and their **names** (the legal `layout_id` values) ????- every placeholder's `idx`, type, and box geometry ????- the theme's font scheme, in particular the **East-Asian (`ea`) face** ??this template is
  221:   Korean, and the `ea` typeface is the one that will actually be used for most of the text ????- **where `#DB0032` lives** ????and this one has teeth. Two possibilities, and they demand
  222:   different renderer code:
  223:   - *In the theme's colour scheme* (e.g. `accent1`) ??the renderer references
  224:     `MSO_THEME_COLOR.ACCENT_1` and the brand is inherited automatically. A future rebrand
  225:     is a one-file change.
  226:   - *Hard-coded in the master XML* ??the renderer must use a literal
  227:     `RGBColor(0xDB, 0x00, 0x32)` and the brand is now duplicated in our code.
  228: 
  229:   `analyze_pptx_master.py` checks both and prints the answer as
  230:   `brand_accent.resolution`. **Read that line first.**
  231: 
  232: **To fill this section in:**
  233: 
  234: ```bash
  235: pip install -e .
  236: python -m aippt.analyze_pptx_master
  237: ```
  238: 
  239: and paste the stdout summary here, replacing this block.
  240: 
  241: > ```
  242: > ?뚢????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????> ?? PASTE THE REAL analyze_pptx_master.py OUTPUT HERE.              ??> ?? Until then, every ??above is an open question and the AI path   ??> ?? cannot be run at all ??there is no layout catalog to inject.    ??> ?붴????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????????> ```
  243: 
  244: ### ?뱰 python-pptx gotchas the renderer must absorb
  245: 
  246: These are documented library behaviours. They are not speculative, and each has already
  247: been written into `renderer.py`'s contract.
  248: 
  249: **1. `add_slide()` must use the template's own layouts.**
  250: ```python
  251: prs = Presentation("Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx")
  252: layout = prs.slide_layouts[N]          # the TEMPLATE's layout
  253: slide = prs.slides.add_slide(layout)   # inherits master, theme, fonts, brand
  254: ```
  255: 
  256: **2. `prs.slide_layouts` only exposes the *first* master's layouts.** If the template has
  257: more than one master, layouts on masters 2+ are invisible through that property. Iterate
  258: `prs.slide_masters[i].slide_layouts` instead. `resolve_layout()` searches all masters for
  259: exactly this reason.
  260: 
  261: **3. Unused placeholders still render.** A layout placeholder that is never filled shows
  262: its prompt text ("Click to add text") to anyone who opens the deck to edit it. Every
  263: placeholder a slide does not use must be explicitly removed:
  264: ```python
  265: ph._element.getparent().remove(ph._element)
  266: ```
  267: Exempt `FOOTER`, `SLIDE_NUMBER` and `DATE` ??those are template furniture and are supposed
  268: to stay.
  269: 
  270: **4. There is no `slides.remove()`.** To strip the template's own example slides you must
  271: drop the relationship *and* unlink the `sldIdLst` entry ??both, in that order. Doing only
  272: the second leaves an orphaned relationship and PowerPoint declares the file corrupt.
  273: 
  274: **5. Placeholder geometry may be `None` on the layout**, inherited from the master.
  275: python-pptx surfaces the `None` rather than resolving it. The analyzer resolves it against
  276: the master and records `geometry_inherited_from_master`; downstream code reads the manifest
  277: and never re-derives this.
  278: 
  279: **6. No SmartArt.** No API, and the `diagramData` part is undocumented and regenerated by
  280: PowerPoint on open. Permanently ruled out. See 짠7.
  281: 
  282: **7. No autofit.** Big enough to be its own section. See 짠5.
  283: 
  284: **8. `text_frame.text = "..."` destroys run formatting.** It collapses the frame to a
  285: single unstyled run. Since 111 inline `<code>` spans depend on run formatting, the renderer
  286: must build paragraphs and runs explicitly, never assign `.text` on a frame that needs
  287: styling.
  288: 
  289: ---
  290: 
  291: ## 5. Text overflow ??the #1 design risk
  292: 
  293: **This is the thing most likely to sink the project, and it deserves to be read twice.**
  294: 
  295: ### The mechanism
  296: 
  297: PowerPoint's "shrink text on overflow" is not a *result* stored in the file. It is an
  298: *instruction* ??`<a:normAutofit fontScale="..."/>` ??that PowerPoint's layout engine
  299: evaluates when it opens the document.
  300: 
  301: python-pptx can write the instruction. It **cannot evaluate it**. It has no font metrics,
  302: no line breaker, and no layout engine. So:
  303: 
  304: ```python
  305: tf.word_wrap = True
  306: tf.auto_size = MSO_AUTO_SIZE.TEXT_TO_FIT_SHAPE   # sets a flag. Fits nothing.
  307: ```
  308: 
  309: Overflowing text does not raise, does not warn, and is not visible in the XML. It simply
  310: renders **off the bottom edge of the slide**. ?뱰
  311: 
  312: The consequence is stark: **every deck this project generates is one verbose LLM sentence
  313: away from a broken slide, and nothing in the stack will tell you.** An LLM writing to a
  314: length that reads well in chat will overflow a 4-inch box essentially every time.
  315: 
  316: ### The Korean multiplier ??
  317: The corpus is Korean. A Hangul syllable is a **full-width** glyph ??roughly **twice** the
  318: advance width of a proportional Latin character. A width model built on Latin metrics
  319: underestimates a Korean line by about 2횞.
  320: 
  321: That single oversight would make every number in the layout catalog wrong, in the dangerous
  322: direction, for most of the actual content. So the budget model carries **two** character
  323: counts (`chars_per_line_latin`, `chars_per_line_cjk`), the prompt states both, and mixed
  324: text is budgeted against the CJK figure. Wrapping differs too: Latin wraps at spaces,
  325: Korean wraps at **any character** ??there are no spaces inside a Hangul clause.
  326: 
  327: ### The three-layer defence
  328: 
  329: **Layer 1 ??PREVENT.** The only layer that scales. Inject the per-placeholder content
  330: budget into the author prompt: *"max 6 bullets 횞 9 words; title ??42 Korean characters."*
  331: A model told a concrete number mostly complies. This is why `analyze_pptx_master.py`
  332: computes content budgets at all ??they exist to be put in a prompt, not to be admired.
  333: 
  334: **Layer 2 ??MEASURE and DEGRADE** (`render/fit.py`). Before writing, estimate the rendered
  335: height. If it exceeds the box, degrade in a fixed order:
  336: 
  337: 1. fits ??write it;
  338: 2. **shrink** (?? steps of 2pt, floor 12pt body / 20pt title ??below that it is unreadable
  339:    from the back of a room, and a legible slide with less on it beats a full slide nobody
  340:    can read);
  341: 3. **tighten** paragraph spacing (buys about one line);
  342: 4. **split** onto a continuation slide ??the *correct* answer for genuinely long content,
  343:    and what a human would do;
  344: 5. **truncate** ??last resort, recorded as a defect, never silent.
  345: 
  346: Explicitly **not** in the ladder: asking an LLM to shorten the text here. That is layer 1's
  347: job, upstream, where the model still has the source material and the intent. Doing it at
  348: render time ??with only the rendered string in hand ??produces a summary of a summary, and
  349: it reads like one.
  350: 
  351: **Layer 3 ??VERIFY.** Layers 1 and 2 both rest on an *estimate* (`LATIN_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO
  352: = 0.52` is a decent guess, not a measurement). The only ground truth is a render: PPTX ??LibreOffice ??PDF ??PNG ??check for ink where ink should not be. This is the authoritative
  353: check and the only thing that will ever catch a bad font-metric assumption. See 짠9.
  354: 
  355: The honest summary: layers 1 and 2 are heuristics, and they will be wrong sometimes. Layer
  356: 3 is why that is survivable.
  357: 
  358: ---
  359: 
  360: ## 6. Images ??
  361: Three in the corpus, all `image/jpeg` data-URIs (**not** PNG ??verified), nested inside
  362: `.photo-desc` ??`.photo-placeholder` ??`.photo-grid`. That nesting is why the adapter uses
  363: BeautifulSoup and not a regex.
  364: 
  365: ```python
  366: header, b64 = data_uri.split(",", 1)     # 'data:image/jpeg;base64'
  367: blob = base64.b64decode(b64)
  368: pic = slide.shapes.add_picture(io.BytesIO(blob), left, top, width=w)
  369: ```
  370: 
  371: `add_picture` accepts any file-like object and sniffs the format from the header, so JPEG
  372: vs PNG needs no branching.
  373: 
  374: **Aspect-fit, never stretch.** This is the part that goes wrong:
  375: 
  376: > Passing **both** `width` and `height` to `add_picture` **distorts the image.**
  377: 
  378: Pass one, let python-pptx derive the other from the intrinsic size, then centre it:
  379: 
  380: ```python
  381: box_ar = box_w / box_h
  382: img_ar = pic.image.size[0] / pic.image.size[1]      # needs Pillow
  383: # img wider than box -> constrain width;  taller -> constrain height
  384: ```
  385: 
  386: `pic.image.size` requires **Pillow**. Without it python-pptx will still place the picture
  387: but cannot read its intrinsic dimensions, so aspect-fit degrades silently into stretch ??and stretched photographs of hardware and people look *wrong* in a way everyone notices and
  388: nobody can immediately name. That is why Pillow is a hard dependency, not an optional one.
  389: 
  390: **Size.** A 619 KB HTML file is ~600 KB of base64, and the IR keeps the data URI verbatim,
  391: so the IR is large but still a single self-contained text file ??no sidecar asset directory
  392: to keep in sync, which matters because the IR is passed to an LLM and round-tripped through
  393: a QA loop. If it ever becomes a problem, `source.kind` already supports `"path"` and
  394: nothing else has to change.
  395: 
  396: ---
  397: 
  398: ## 7. Diagrams ??a hybrid, and why neither pure option wins
  399: 
  400: The corpus has 3 `.mermaid` blocks (1 `sequenceDiagram`, 2 flowcharts) and 7 `<svg>`
  401: elements. Small in count, but diagrams carry the highest information density per slide in
  402: the whole corpus ??these are architecture decks, and the architecture *is* the diagram.
  403: 
  404: **SmartArt is out.** No python-pptx API; the `diagramData` part is undocumented,
  405: version-sensitive, and regenerated by PowerPoint on open. Hand-writing it is not a shortcut,
  406: it is a permanent maintenance liability. ?뱰
  407: 
  408: The two viable options fail in *different* ways, which is exactly why the answer is neither:
  409: 
  410: | | Native autoshapes | Rasterised (mermaid-cli ??PNG) |
  411: |---|---|---|
  412: | Editable | ??| ??flat image |
  413: | On-brand (theme colours) | ??| ??mermaid's palette |
  414: | Crisp when resized | ??vector | ??goes soft |
  415: | Handles arbitrary graphs | ??needs a layout engine | ??|
  416: | External dependency | none | Node + `mmdc` |
  417: 
  418: ### Recommendation: decide per diagram, by complexity
  419: 
  420: - **??8 nodes, ??10 edges, acyclic ??native.** Layered (Sugiyama-lite) layout, rounded
  421:   rectangles, elbow connectors bound to the shapes with `begin_connect`/`end_connect` so
  422:   they stay attached when a human drags a box. That reattachment is most of the value of
  423:   going native at all.
  424: - **Anything else ??rasterise.** And this is honest, not a cop-out: an unreadable native
  425:   approximation of a 20-node graph is strictly worse than a clean picture of it.
  426: - **Parse failure ??rasterise.** `parse_mermaid` handles the subset the corpus actually
  427:   uses and raises `UnsupportedDiagram` on anything else. It must never *half*-parse: a
  428:   partially-understood diagram renders as a **wrong** diagram, and a wrong architecture
  429:   diagram in an engineering deck is a real cost ??worse than an honest fallback.
  430: - **Author override.** `render_hint: native | image | auto` in the IR. The person who wrote
  431:   the diagram knows things a node count does not.
  432: 
  433: The corpus's own diagrams are 4?? nodes and one 3-participant sequence diagram ??so in
  434: practice **most of them go native**, and the raster path is the safety net rather than the
  435: default.
  436: 
  437: ---
  438: 
  439: ## 8. Prompt engineering for decks
  440: 
  441: Four decisions, in `prompts/`.
  442: 
  443: **The invariant is stated first, and as a rule about the model's job**, not a formatting
  444: preference: *"You produce structured content. You never produce design."* It is also
  445: enforced structurally ??the schema has nowhere to put a coordinate ??but a model that
  446: understands *why* complies more consistently than one merely blocked from misbehaving.
  447: 
  448: **Budgets are injected, and framed as physics.** The catalog goes into the system prompt
  449: verbatim, carrying the legal `layout_id` values and the per-placeholder budgets. The prompt
  450: tells the model *why* the ceiling is hard: **there is no autofit; text that overflows is
  451: silently lost.** And it asks for **80% of budget**, because bold runs, inline code and wide
  452: punctuation all eat width the estimate does not model.
  453: 
  454: **Two stages, because one call cannot do both jobs.** A single call that must hold the
  455: deck's narrative arc *and* respect 30 per-placeholder character budgets does neither well ??it drifts on structure and overflows on length.
  456: 
  457: - Stage 1 (`10_outline_storyboard.md`): topic + sources ??storyboard. Decides *the story*
  458:   and picks the layouts, at deck level, where the model can see the whole shape and avoid
  459:   emitting nine consecutive bullet slides.
  460: - Stage 2 (`20_slide_fill.md`): one outline entry ??one slide's IR. Runs per-slide, in
  461:   parallel, and is injected with **only that layout's budget** ??narrowing the context
  462:   measurably improves adherence, because the model is no longer choosing a layout, it is
  463:   filling a box whose exact dimensions it has just been told.
  464: 
  465: **Structured output, not free-text-then-parse.** "Return JSON" yields JSON *most* of the
  466: time, and the failures are the expensive kind ??a stray markdown fence, a trailing comma, a
  467: hallucinated field. All three providers can constrain output to a schema natively (Claude:
  468: tool use with `input_schema` + forced `tool_choice`; OpenAI: Structured Outputs with
  469: `strict: true`; Gemini: `response_schema`). Use it, and get a hard guarantee instead of a
  470: soft one.
  471: 
  472: Two provider caveats that *will* bite, documented in `ai_adapter.py`: OpenAI's `strict`
  473: mode rejects `oneOf`, `default` and `format: uri`, and Gemini accepts only an OpenAPI-3
  474: subset with no `$ref`. Both need a **derived** variant of the schema, generated by a tested
  475: transform ??never a second hand-maintained copy, which will drift from the canonical one and
  476: do so invisibly.
  477: 
  478: **Repairs are bounded and specific.** On validation failure, re-prompt once with the exact
  479: error ??`slides[3].blocks[1].rows[2]: row has 3 cells but the table declares 4 columns`.
  480: A precise, machine-generated message repairs on the first retry the overwhelming majority
  481: of the time; "invalid JSON" does not. After `max_repairs`, **fail loud**. Never silently
  482: drop a slide: an 11-slide deck that was asked for 12 is a defect nobody will notice.
  483: 
  484: ---
  485: 
  486: ## 9. The quality loop
  487: 
  488: ```
  489: deck.pptx ?> soffice --headless --convert-to pdf ?> pdftoppm -png ?> slide-NN.png
  490:                                                                           ??                          ?뚢?????????????????????????????????????????????????                          ??                                              ??              deterministic geometry checks                    vision critique
  491:               (fit.detect_overflow_in_png)                     (30_visual_qa.md)
  492:                           ??                                              ??                          ?붴????????????????verdict ?꾟????????????????????????                                    pass / fix / regenerate
  493: ```
  494: 
  495: **Note:** LibreOffice's `--convert-to png` renders only the *first* slide. Go via PDF.
  496: 
  497: **The division of labour is the point.** Geometry is checked with **arithmetic**: ink near
  498: a slide edge, an ink bounding box taller than its declared placeholder, two placeholders'
  499: ink overlapping. A vision model will confidently miss a 3-pixel overhang; arithmetic will
  500: not. The vision model is then asked only to judge what arithmetic cannot name ??legibility
  501: at projection distance, density, whether a diagram's edge labels have collapsed into each
  502: other, whether an image is subtly stretched.
  503: 
  504: Three verdicts, with sharply different costs:
  505: 
  506: - **`pass`** ??ships. Minor defects still pass; perfection is not the bar.
  507: - **`fix`** ??deterministic repair (shrink / split / re-fit). No model call. `fit.py` does it.
  508: - **`regenerate`** ??the *content* is wrong: too much of it, or the wrong block type. This
  509:   is the only verdict that costs another generation call, so the prompt reserves it.
  510: 
  511: The critic is explicitly instructed that **when in doubt, pass** ??a false positive costs a
  512: pointless regeneration cycle, and a critic that flags everything is indistinguishable from
  513: one that flags nothing.
  514: 
  515: ---
  516: 
  517: ## 10. Roadmap
  518: 
  519: | Phase | Scope | Gate |
  520: |---|---|---|
  521: | **0 ??Ground truth** | Run `analyze_pptx_master.py`. Fill in 짠4. | `layout_manifest.json` has real data |
  522: | **1 ??HTML ??IR ??PPTX** | `html_adapter` + `renderer` + `fit`. **No AI.** | All 30 corpus slides round-trip; `ConversionReport.lossy` is empty except for the known `sim-*` / `<svg>` cases |
  523: | **2 ??AI author** | `ai_adapter`, both prompt stages, structured output, bounded retry | A deck generated from a topic opens in PowerPoint, is on-brand, and nothing overflows |
  524: | **3 ??Visual QA loop** | Render ??PNG ??geometry checks ??vision critique ??repair | The loop converges without a human |
  525: 
  526: **Phase 1 has no AI in it, on purpose.** It is the highest-value phase and the cheapest to
  527: debug. If a deck we *already have* cannot survive the round trip, no amount of prompt
  528: engineering will save the AI path. And a Phase 1 failure is debuggable in a way that "the
  529: model wrote something odd" never is.
  530: 
  531: It also produces something immediately useful on its own: a converter for 30 slides of
  532: existing content that currently cannot be handed to anyone in PowerPoint form.
  533: 
  534: ---
  535: 
  536: ## 11. Open questions and risks
  537: 
  538: ### Blocking
  539: 
  540: **R1. The PoC has never run.** ??Nothing about the template is confirmed, and ??worse ??the analyzer has never had a Python interpreter look at it, so it may not even import.
  541: Expect to fix a bug or two. *Mitigation: run it. It is two seconds and one command.*
  542: 
  543: **R2. Where does `#DB0032` live?** ??Theme slot or hard-coded in the master? Changes what
  544: the renderer does for every inline `code` run, every callout, and every native diagram
  545: node. `analyze_pptx_master.py` answers this directly. *Read `brand_accent.resolution` first.*
  546: 
  547: **R3. Does the template have usable layouts at all?** ??A corporate template with only
  548: "Title" and "Blank" would break the layout-driven design and force a fallback to
  549: free-floating shapes on a blank layout ??losing most of the master's benefit. Unlikely,
  550: but it is the assumption the whole architecture rests on, and it is unverified.
  551: 
  552: ### Structural
  553: 
  554: **R4. Text overflow.** The #1 risk. See 짠5. Mitigated in three layers, none of which is
  555: airtight on its own.
  556: 
  557: **R5. The font-metric model is a guess.** `LATIN_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO = 0.52` is a reasonable
  558: constant, not a measurement of the template's actual typeface. The 10% safety margin is a
  559: cushion, not a proof. *Mitigation: layer 3 (render ??PNG) is the ground truth; calibrate
  560: the constants against a real render once and revisit.*
  561: 
  562: **R6. `analyze_pptx_master.py` and `fit.py` must agree.** If the two width models diverge,
  563: the budget the LLM is given stops matching the budget the renderer enforces, and layer 1
  564: silently stops working. *Mitigation: `fit.py` imports the constants from the analyzer rather
  565: than restating them. Do not "clean this up" by re-declaring them.*
  566: 
  567: **R7. HTML ??layout mapping is invented, not derived.** The HTML has no notion of a
  568: PowerPoint layout, so `choose_layout()` guesses from content shape. It will get some slides
  569: wrong. *Mitigation: rule-based and inspectable; `ConversionReport` surfaces every decision;
  570: tune against the 30-slide corpus, which is exactly what a corpus is for.*
  571: 
  572: **R8. Lossy constructs.** `sim-*` interactive panels and the 7 hand-drawn `<svg>` diagrams
  573: have no faithful PPTX representation. *Mitigation: warn loudly, never drop silently. A
  574: slide that renders beautifully having quietly lost its only diagram is worse than a loud
  575: failure.*
  576: 
  577: ### Open questions
  578: 
  579: - **Does the deck ship the template's example slides?** `strip_existing=True` by default ??  they are the template's *examples*, not our content ??but this should be confirmed with
  580:   whoever owns the template.
  581: - **What is the review workflow?** A generated deck is a draft. Who approves it, and does
  582:   the QA loop's verdict gate anything, or merely advise?
  583: - **Speaker notes.** The IR carries them and they are free of any budget. Should the AI
  584:   author populate them by default? Argument for: it is the natural home for the nuance that
  585:   had to be cut from the slide, and it costs little. *Recommendation: yes, by default.*
  586: - **Which model for which stage?** The outline stage rewards reasoning about narrative arc;
  587:   the fill stage rewards instruction-following against a hard budget. They may not want the
  588:   same model. Worth measuring rather than assuming.

   77: BULLET_GAP_RATIO = 0.35         # space-before between bullets, in font-size units

   86: def emu_to_in(emu: int | None) -> float | None:
   87:     return None if emu is None else round(emu / EMU_PER_INCH, 4)
   88: 
   89: 
   90: def emu_to_pt(emu: int | None) -> float | None:
   91:     return None if emu is None else round(emu / EMU_PER_PT, 2)

   94: def aspect_label(w: int, h: int) -> str:
   95:     """Name the aspect ratio, tolerating the rounding real templates contain."""
   96:     if not w or not h:
   97:         return "unknown"
   98:     r = w / h
   99:     for label, target in (("16:9", 16 / 9), ("16:10", 16 / 10), ("4:3", 4 / 3), ("3:2", 3 / 2)):
  100:         if abs(r - target) < 0.02:
  101:             return label
  102:     return f"{r:.3f}:1"
  103: 
  104: 
  105: def _rels_of(part: Any) -> list[Any]:
  106:     """python-pptx has changed the Relationships type across versions; cope with both."""
  107:     rels = part.rels
  108:     try:
  109:         return list(rels.values())
  110:     except AttributeError:
  111:         return [rels[k] for k in rels]
  112: 
  113: 
  114: def _element_of(obj: Any) -> Any:
  115:     """Raw lxml element behind a python-pptx object (private API, hence the fallback)."""
  116:     for attr in ("_element", "element"):
  117:         el = getattr(obj, attr, None)
  118:         if el is not None:
  119:             return el
  120:     raise AttributeError(f"cannot reach the XML element of {obj!r}")

  136: def _typefaces(font_el: Any) -> dict[str, str | None]:
  137:     """latin / ea (East Asian -- the Korean face) / cs typefaces of a font scheme half."""
  138:     if font_el is None:
  139:         return {}
  140:     out: dict[str, str | None] = {}
  141:     for key in ("latin", "ea", "cs"):
  142:         el = font_el.find(f"{A}{key}")
  143:         out[key] = el.get("typeface") if el is not None else None
  144:     return out
  145: 
  146: 
  147: def _slot_color(slot_el: Any) -> str | None:
  148:     """Resolve one ``a:clrScheme`` slot to an RRGGBB string."""
  149:     if slot_el is None:
  150:         return None
  151:     srgb = slot_el.find(f"{A}srgbClr")
  152:     if srgb is not None and srgb.get("val"):
  153:         return srgb.get("val").upper()
  154:     sysclr = slot_el.find(f"{A}sysClr")
  155:     if sysclr is not None:
  156:         # sysClr carries the resolved value in @lastClr; @val is a system name (e.g. windowText)
  157:         return (sysclr.get("lastClr") or sysclr.get("val") or "").upper() or None
  158:     return None
  159: 
  160: 
  161: def read_theme(master: Any) -> ThemeInfo:
  162:     """Pull the font scheme + colour map off the theme part related to this master.
  163: 
  164:     python-pptx exposes no theme API, so we walk the OPC relationships by hand.
  165:     Returns an empty-ish ThemeInfo rather than raising if the theme is unreachable --
  166:     a missing theme must not sink the whole analysis.
  167:     """
  168:     info = ThemeInfo()
  169:     theme_el = None
  170:     for rel in _rels_of(master.part):
  171:         if str(rel.reltype).endswith("/theme"):
  172:             try:
  173:                 from lxml import etree
  174:                 theme_el = etree.fromstring(rel.target_part.blob)
  175:             except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - defensive
  176:                 theme_el = None
  177:             break
  178:     if theme_el is None:
  179:         return info
  180: 
  181:     info.name = theme_el.get("name")
  182:     scheme = theme_el.find(f"{A}themeElements")
  183:     if scheme is None:
  184:         return info
  185: 
  186:     font_scheme = scheme.find(f"{A}fontScheme")
  187:     if font_scheme is not None:
  188:         info.major_font = _typefaces(font_scheme.find(f"{A}majorFont"))
  189:         info.minor_font = _typefaces(font_scheme.find(f"{A}minorFont"))
  190: 
  191:     clr_scheme = scheme.find(f"{A}clrScheme")
  192:     if clr_scheme is not None:
  193:         for slot in CLR_SLOTS:
  194:             value = _slot_color(clr_scheme.find(f"{A}{slot}"))
  195:             if value:
  196:                 info.color_map[slot] = value
  197:                 if value == BRAND_ACCENT:
  198:                     info.brand_accent_slots.append(slot)
  199:     return info
  200: 
  201: 
  202: def find_brand_accent(master: Any, theme: ThemeInfo) -> dict[str, Any]:
  203:     """Answer the brief's question: *where does #DB0032 actually live?*
  204: 
  205:     Checks, in order of authority:
  206:       1. the theme colour scheme (the answer we want -- means the renderer can just say
  207:          ``MSO_THEME_COLOR.ACCENT_n`` and inherit the brand automatically);
  208:       2. hard-coded ``srgbClr val="DB0032"`` anywhere in the master's own XML.
  209:     """

  214:     }
  215:     try:
  216:         master_el = _element_of(master)

  218:             if (el.get("val") or "").upper() == BRAND_ACCENT:
  219:                 hits["hardcoded_in_master_xml"] += 1
  220:     except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - defensive

  241: TITLE_TYPES = {"TITLE", "CENTER_TITLE", "VERTICAL_TITLE"}
  242: BODY_TYPES = {"BODY", "SUBTITLE", "OBJECT", "VERTICAL_BODY", "VERTICAL_OBJECT"}

  244: 
  245: def _lvl1_size_pt(el: Any) -> float | None:
  246:     """First ``a:lvl1pPr/a:defRPr@sz`` under ``el``. ``sz`` is in 1/100 pt (4400 == 44pt)."""
  247:     if el is None:
  248:         return None
  249:     for lvl in el.iter(f"{A}lvl1pPr"):
  250:         rpr = lvl.find(f"{A}defRPr")
  251:         if rpr is not None and rpr.get("sz"):
  252:             try:
  253:                 return int(rpr.get("sz")) / 100.0
  254:             except ValueError:
  255:                 return None
  256:     return None
  257: 
  258: 
  259: def _txstyles_size_pt(master: Any, ph_type: str) -> float | None:
  260:     """Master-level ``p:txStyles`` default for this class of placeholder."""
  261:     try:
  262:         master_el = _element_of(master)
  263:     except AttributeError:
  264:         return None
  265:     tx = master_el.find(f"{P}txStyles")
  266:     if tx is None:
  267:         return None
  268:     if ph_type in TITLE_TYPES:
  269:         style = tx.find(f"{P}titleStyle")
  270:     elif ph_type in BODY_TYPES:
  271:         style = tx.find(f"{P}bodyStyle")
  272:     else:
  273:         style = tx.find(f"{P}otherStyle")
  274:     return _lvl1_size_pt(style)
  275:

  277: def resolve_font_pt(layout_ph: Any, master_ph: Any, master: Any, ph_type: str) -> tuple[float, str]:
  278:     """Effective lvl-1 font size for a placeholder, plus *where the number came from*.
  279: 
  280:     Inheritance chain, most specific first -- this mirrors what PowerPoint itself does:
  281:     layout placeholder list-style -> matching master placeholder list-style ->
  282:     master ``p:txStyles`` -> hard default.
  283: 
  284:     Returns ``(points, source)``. ``source`` is recorded in the manifest so a reader can
  285:     distinguish a real template value from our fallback guess.
  286:     """
  287:     for obj, source in ((layout_ph, "layout"), (master_ph, "master-placeholder")):
  288:         if obj is None:
  289:             continue
  290:         try:
  291:             size = _lvl1_size_pt(_element_of(obj))
  292:         except AttributeError:
  293:             size = None
  294:         if size:
  295:             return size, source
  296: 
  297:     size = _txstyles_size_pt(master, ph_type)
  298:     if size:
  299:         return size, "master-txStyles"
  300: 
  301:     if ph_type in TITLE_TYPES:
  302:         return DEFAULT_PT["title"], "default-guess"
  303:     if ph_type in BODY_TYPES:
  304:         return DEFAULT_PT["body"], "default-guess"
  305:     return DEFAULT_PT["other"], "default-guess"
  306: 
  307: 
  308: # [REDACTED]
  309: # Content budget
  310: # [REDACTED]
  311: 
  312: def content_budget(width_emu: int | None, height_emu: int | None, font_pt: float,
  313:                    ph_type: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
  314:     """How much text actually fits in this box -- the number we hand to the LLM.
  315: 
  316:     This is a *typographic estimate*, not a rendering. It exists because python-pptx
  317:     cannot autofit and PowerPoint will happily let text spill off the slide. The model
  318:     is deliberately simple and conservative:
  319: 
  320:         chars_per_line = box_width_pt / (font_pt * char_width_ratio) * SAFETY
  321:         lines          = box_height_pt / (font_pt * LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO)
  322: 
  323:     Two character budgets are reported because this corpus is bilingual: a Hangul glyph
  324:     is ~2x the advance width of a Latin one, so a box that holds 60 Latin characters
  325:     holds only ~31 Korean ones. Mixed text should be budgeted against ``cjk``.
  326: 
  327:     Titles are reported as a flat character cap (they get 1-2 lines). Body placeholders
  328:     are reported as ``max_bullets x max_words_per_bullet``, which is the shape the
  329:     prompt actually needs.
  330:     """
  331:     if not width_emu or not height_emu or not font_pt:
  332:         return {"computable": False,
  333:                 "reason": "placeholder has no resolved geometry or font size"}
  334: 
  335:     w_pt = width_emu / EMU_PER_PT
  336:     h_pt = height_emu / EMU_PER_PT
  337: 
  338:     latin_cpl = max(1, int(w_pt / (font_pt * LATIN_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO) * SAFETY))
  339:     cjk_cpl = max(1, int(w_pt / (font_pt * CJK_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO) * SAFETY))
  340:     max_lines = max(1, int(h_pt / (font_pt * LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO)))
  341: 
  342:     budget: dict[str, Any] = {
  343:         "computable": True,
  344:         "font_pt": font_pt,
  345:         "chars_per_line_latin": latin_cpl,
  346:         "chars_per_line_cjk": cjk_cpl,
  347:         "max_lines": max_lines,
  348:     }
  349: 
  350:     if ph_type in TITLE_TYPES:
  351:         # A title that wraps to 3 lines is a design failure; cap the usable lines at 2.
  352:         title_lines = min(2, max_lines)
  353:         budget["max_title_chars_latin"] = latin_cpl * title_lines
  354:         budget["max_title_chars_cjk"] = cjk_cpl * title_lines
  355:         budget["recommended_lines"] = title_lines
  356:     elif ph_type in BODY_TYPES:
  357:         # Each bullet costs its own line plus the space-before gap.
  358:         per_bullet = LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO + BULLET_GAP_RATIO
  359:         max_bullets = max(1, int(h_pt / (font_pt * per_bullet)))
  360:         budget["max_bullets"] = max_bullets
  361:         budget["max_words_per_bullet"] = max(1, int(latin_cpl / LATIN_CHARS_PER_WORD))
  362:         budget["max_chars_per_bullet_latin"] = latin_cpl
  363:         budget["max_chars_per_bullet_cjk"] = cjk_cpl
  364:         budget["note"] = "one line per bullet; a bullet that wraps consumes another slot"
  365:     return budget
  366: 
  367: 
  368: # [REDACTED]
  369: # Semantic role
  370: # [REDACTED]
  371: 
  372: #: Layout names are authored by humans and this template is Korean; match both languages.
  373: ROLE_NAME_HINTS = (
  374:     ("section-divider", ("section", "divider", "chapter", "援щ텇", "媛꾩?", "?뱀뀡")),
  375:     ("agenda", ("agenda", "contents", "toc", "紐⑹감", "李⑤?", "媛쒖슂")),
  376:     ("title", ("title slide", "?쒕ぉ", "?쒖?", "cover")),
  377:     ("closing", ("thank", "end", "closing", "媛먯궗", "留덈Т由?)),
  378:     ("quote", ("quote", "?몄슜")),
  379:     ("comparison", ("comparison", "compare", "鍮꾧탳")),
  380: )
  381: 
  382: 
  383: def suggest_role(layout_name: str, ph_types: list[str]) -> str:
  384:     """Best guess at what a layout is *for*, so the AI can pick one by intent.
  385: 
  386:     Name hints win when present (a human named it), otherwise fall back to placeholder
  387:     composition. This is a suggestion, not a contract -- a human should sanity-check the
  388:     catalog once and correct any row that is wrong.
  389:     """
  390:     lname = (layout_name or "").strip().lower()
  391:     for role, needles in ROLE_NAME_HINTS:
  392:         if any(n in lname for n in needles):
  393:             return role
  394: 
  395:     content = [t for t in ph_types if t not in ("SLIDE_NUMBER", "FOOTER", "DATE", "HEADER")]
  396:     titles = [t for t in content if t in TITLE_TYPES]
  397:     bodies = [t for t in content if t in BODY_TYPES]
  398: 
  399:     if not content:
  400:         return "blank"
  401:     if "PICTURE" in content or "BITMAP" in content:
  402:         return "picture"
  403:     if "TABLE" in content:
  404:         return "table"
  405:     if "CHART" in content:
  406:         return "chart"
  407:     if "CENTER_TITLE" in content or "SUBTITLE" in content:
  408:         return "title"
  409:     if titles and not bodies:
  410:         return "title-only"
  411:     if titles and len(bodies) >= 3:
  412:         return "multi-content"
  413:     if titles and len(bodies) == 2:
  414:         return "two-content"
  415:     if titles and len(bodies) == 1:
  416:         return "bullets"
  417:     return "custom"
  418: 
  419: 
  420: # [REDACTED]
  421: # Extraction
  422: # [REDACTED]
  423: 
  424: def _ph_type_name(ph: Any) -> str:
  425:     try:
  426:         t = ph.placeholder_format.type
  427:     except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - defensive
  428:         return "UNKNOWN"
  429:     if t is None:
  430:         return "UNKNOWN"
  431:     # PP_PLACEHOLDER enum members stringify as e.g. "BODY (2)" depending on version.
  432:     return getattr(t, "name", None) or str(t).split()[0]
  433: 
  434: 
  435: def _master_ph_for(master: Any, idx: int, ph_type: str) -> Any:
  436:     """The master placeholder a layout placeholder inherits from: match idx, then type."""
  437:     candidates = list(master.placeholders)
  438:     for ph in candidates:
  439:         if ph.placeholder_format.idx == idx:
  440:             return ph

  442:         if _ph_type_name(ph) == ph_type:

  446: 
  447: def extract_placeholder(layout_ph: Any, master: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
  448:     """One placeholder -> its manifest row, geometry resolved and budget computed."""
  449:     fmt = layout_ph.placeholder_format
  450:     idx = fmt.idx
  451:     ph_type = _ph_type_name(layout_ph)
  452:     master_ph = _master_ph_for(master, idx, ph_type)
  453: 
  454:     # Geometry may be absent on the layout and inherited from the master. python-pptx
  455:     # surfaces that as None rather than resolving it, so resolve it ourselves.
  456:     geom: dict[str, int | None] = {}
  457:     inherited = False
  458:     for key in ("left", "top", "width", "height"):
  459:         value = getattr(layout_ph, key, None)
  460:         if value is None and master_ph is not None:
  461:             value = getattr(master_ph, key, None)
  462:             if value is not None:
  463:                 inherited = True
  464:         geom[key] = None if value is None else int(value)
  465: 
  466:     font_pt, font_src = resolve_font_pt(layout_ph, master_ph, master, ph_type)
  467: 
  468:     return {
  469:         "idx": idx,
  470:         "type": ph_type,
  471:         "name": layout_ph.name,
  472:         "geometry_inherited_from_master": inherited,
  473:         "emu": dict(geom),
  474:         "inches": {k: emu_to_in(v) for k, v in geom.items()},
  475:         "font_pt": font_pt,
  476:         "font_pt_source": font_src,
  477:         "content_budget": content_budget(geom["width"], geom["height"], font_pt, ph_type),
  478:     }
  479: 
  480: 
  481: def extract_layout(index: int, layout: Any, master: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
  482:     placeholders = [extract_placeholder(ph, master) for ph in layout.placeholders]
  483:     # A None idx would make a plain (is None, idx) key compare None < None -> TypeError.
  484:     placeholders.sort(key=lambda p: (p["idx"] is None, p["idx"] if p["idx"] is not None else -1))
  485:     ph_types = [p["type"] for p in placeholders]
  486: 
  487:     # Shapes the template itself draws (logo bars, rules). They render on every slide
  488:     # using this layout and are NOT ours to fill -- but they DO eat visual space.
  489:     decorations = [s.name for s in layout.shapes if not s.is_placeholder]
  490: 
  491:     return {
  492:         "index": index,
  493:         "layout_id": layout.name,
  494:         "name": layout.name,
  495:         "semantic_role": suggest_role(layout.name, ph_types),
  496:         "placeholder_count": len(placeholders),
  497:         "decoration_shapes": decorations,
  498:         "placeholders": placeholders,
  499:     }
  500: 
  501: 
  502: def analyze(template: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
  503:     """Open the template read-only and return the full manifest dict."""
  504:     from pptx import Presentation
  505: 
  506:     raw = template.read_bytes()
  507:     digest = hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()
  508:     # BytesIO => python-pptx never touches the file on disk. Read-only, guaranteed.
  509:     prs = Presentation(io.BytesIO(raw))
  510: 
  511:     width, height = int(prs.slide_width), int(prs.slide_height)
  512: 
  513:     masters: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
  514:     for mi, master in enumerate(prs.slide_masters):
  515:         theme = read_theme(master)
  516:         masters.append({
  517:             "index": mi,
  518:             "name": master.name,
  519:             "theme_name": theme.name,
  520:             "font_scheme": {"major": theme.major_font, "minor": theme.minor_font},
  521:             "color_map": theme.color_map,
  522:             "brand_accent": find_brand_accent(master, theme),
  523:             "layout_count": len(master.slide_layouts),
  524:             "layouts": [extract_layout(li, lay, master)
  525:                         for li, lay in enumerate(master.slide_layouts)],
  526:         })
  527: 
  528:     existing = [{"index": i, "layout": s.slide_layout.name, "shape_count": len(s.shapes)}
  529:                 for i, s in enumerate(prs.slides)]
  530: 
  531:     return {
  532:         "schema_version": "1.0",
  533:         "generated_by": "aippt.analyze_pptx_master",
  534:         "source": {
  535:             "filename": template.name,
  536:             "sha256": digest,
  537:             "bytes": len(raw),
  538:         },
  539:         "deck": {
  540:             "slide_width_emu": width,
  541:             "slide_height_emu": height,
  542:             "slide_width_in": emu_to_in(width),
  543:             "slide_height_in": emu_to_in(height),
  544:             "aspect_ratio": aspect_label(width, height),
  545:             "master_count": len(prs.slide_masters),
  546:             "layout_count_first_master": len(prs.slide_layouts),
  547:             "existing_slide_count": len(prs.slides),
  548:         },
  549:         "masters": masters,
  550:         "existing_slides": existing,
  551:         "budget_model": {
  552:             "latin_char_width_ratio": LATIN_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO,
  553:             "cjk_char_width_ratio": CJK_CHAR_WIDTH_RATIO,
  554:             "line_height_ratio": LINE_HEIGHT_RATIO,
  555:             "safety_factor": SAFETY,
  556:             "caveat": "typographic estimate, not a render; the render->PNG check is authoritative",
  557:         },
  558:     }
  559: 
  560: 
  561: # [REDACTED]
  562: # Rendering the artifacts
  563: # [REDACTED]
  564: 
  565: def render_catalog(manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
  566:     """The prompt-context artifact: everything the AI author needs, nothing it doesn't.
  567: 
  568:     Kept deliberately compact -- this text is pasted into every generation call, so every
  569:     token in it is paid for on every slide.
  570:     """
  571:     deck = manifest["deck"]
  572:     out: list[str] = []
  573:     w = out.append
  574: 
  575:     w("# Layout Catalog")
  576:     w("")
  577:     w(f"Generated from `{manifest['source']['filename']}` "
  578:       f"(sha256 `{manifest['source']['sha256'][:12]}...`) by `analyze_pptx_master.py`.")
  579:     w("**Do not hand-edit** -- re-run the analyzer instead.")
  580:     w("")
  581:     w(f"- Slide size: **{deck['slide_width_in']}in x {deck['slide_height_in']}in** "
  582:       f"({deck['aspect_ratio']}), {deck['slide_width_emu']} x {deck['slide_height_emu']} EMU")
  583:     w(f"- Masters: **{deck['master_count']}** | "
  584:       f"Layouts (first master): **{deck['layout_count_first_master']}** | "
  585:       f"Existing slides: **{deck['existing_slide_count']}**")
  586:     w("")
  587: 
  588:     for master in manifest["masters"]:
  589:         w(f"## Master {master['index']}: {master['name']}")
  590:         w("")
  591:         major = master["font_scheme"].get("major") or {}
  592:         minor = master["font_scheme"].get("minor") or {}
  593:         w(f"- Theme: `{master['theme_name']}`")
  594:         w(f"- Major font (headings): latin `{major.get('latin')}` / east-asian `{major.get('ea')}`")
  595:         w(f"- Minor font (body): latin `{minor.get('latin')}` / east-asian `{minor.get('ea')}`")
  596:         w(f"- Brand accent `#{BRAND_ACCENT}`: {master['brand_accent']['resolution']}")
  597:         if master["color_map"]:
  598:             pairs = ", ".join(f"{k}=#{v}" for k, v in master["color_map"].items())
  599:             w(f"- Colour map: {pairs}")
  600:         w("")
  601:         w("### Layouts")
  602:         w("")
  603:         w("| # | layout_id | role | placeholders |")
  604:         w("|---|-----------|------|--------------|")
  605:         for lay in master["layouts"]:
  606:             types = ", ".join(f"{p['type']}(idx {p['idx']})" for p in lay["placeholders"]) or "-none-"
  607:             w(f"| {lay['index']} | `{lay['layout_id']}` | {lay['semantic_role']} | {types} |")
  608:         w("")
  609: 
  610:         w("### Content budgets")
  611:         w("")
  612:         w("The AI author MUST respect these. `cjk` applies to Korean text, `latin` to English;")
  613:         w("for mixed text, budget against `cjk`.")
  614:         w("")
  615:         for lay in master["layouts"]:
  616:             if not lay["placeholders"]:
  617:                 continue
  618:             w(f"**`{lay['layout_id']}`** ({lay['semantic_role']})")
  619:             w("")
  620:             for ph in lay["placeholders"]:
  621:                 b = ph["content_budget"]
  622:                 head = f"- `idx {ph['idx']}` {ph['type']} -- {ph['inches']['width']}x{ph['inches']['height']}in @ {ph['font_pt']}pt"
  623:                 if not b.get("computable"):
  624:                     w(head + f" -- budget unavailable ({b.get('reason')})")
  625:                     continue
  626:                 if "max_title_chars_cjk" in b:
  627:                     w(head + f" -- max **{b['max_title_chars_latin']} latin chars** "
  628:                              f"/ **{b['max_title_chars_cjk']} korean chars**, "
  629:                              f"<= {b['recommended_lines']} lines")
  630:                 elif "max_bullets" in b:
  631:                     w(head + f" -- max **{b['max_bullets']} bullets** x "
  632:                              f"**{b['max_words_per_bullet']} words** "
  633:                              f"({b['max_chars_per_bullet_latin']} latin / "
  634:                              f"{b['max_chars_per_bullet_cjk']} korean chars per bullet)")
  635:                 else:
  636:                     w(head + f" -- {b['chars_per_line_latin']} latin chars/line, "
  637:                              f"{b['max_lines']} lines")
  638:             w("")
  639: 
  640:     w("---")
  641:     w("")
  642:     w("## Allowed `layout_id` values")
  643:     w("")
  644:     w("The IR `layout_id` field MUST be exactly one of:")
  645:     w("")
  646:     for master in manifest["masters"]:
  647:         for lay in master["layouts"]:
  648:             w(f"- `{lay['layout_id']}`  ({lay['semantic_role']})")
  649:     w("")
  650:     return "\n".join(out) + "\n"
  651:

  655:     print("=" * 78)

  658:     print(f"  slide size : {deck['slide_width_in']}in x {deck['slide_height_in']}in  "
  659:           f"({deck['aspect_ratio']})  [{deck['slide_width_emu']} x {deck['slide_height_emu']} EMU]")
  660:     print(f"  masters    : {deck['master_count']}")

  714: DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME = "Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx"
  715: 
  716: 
  717: def find_default_template(start: Path) -> Path | None:
  718:     """Look for the template beside the repo, then walk up. Keeps the no-arg case working."""
  719:     for base in (start, *start.parents):
  720:         candidate = base / DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME
  721:         if candidate.is_file():
  722:             return candidate
  723:     return None
  724: 
  725: 
  726: def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
  727:     here = Path(__file__).resolve()
  728:     repo_root = here.parents[2]          # .../src/aippt/x.py -> repo root
  729: 
  730:     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
  731:         description="Extract layouts, theme and content budgets from the PPTX master.")
  732:     parser.add_argument("--template", type=Path, default=None,
  733:                         help=f"path to the .pptx (default: nearest {DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME})")
  734:     parser.add_argument("--out-dir", type=Path, default=repo_root / "reference",
  735:                         help="where to write layout_manifest.json / layout_catalog.md")
  736:     parser.add_argument("--quiet", action="store_true", help="suppress the stdout summary")
  737:     args = parser.parse_args(argv)
  738: 
  739:     # Korean layout names and Hangul in the summary must survive a Windows console.
  740:     try:
  741:         sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
  742:     except (AttributeError, ValueError):  # pragma: no cover - very old / redirected stdout
  743:         pass
  744: 
  745:     template = args.template or find_default_template(repo_root)
  746:     if template is None:
  747:         print(f"ERROR: could not find {DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME}. Pass --template <path>.",
  748:               file=sys.stderr)
  749:         return 2
  750:     template = Path(template).expanduser().resolve()
  751:     if not template.is_file():
  752:         print(f"ERROR: template not found: {template}", file=sys.stderr)
  753:         return 2
  754: 
  755:     try:
  756:         import pptx  # noqa: F401
  757:     except ImportError:
  758:         print("ERROR: python-pptx is not installed.  pip install -r requirements.txt",
  759:               file=sys.stderr)
  760:         return 3
  761: 
  762:     try:
  763:         manifest = analyze(template)
  764:     except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - top-level CLI guard, we want the message
  765:         print(f"ERROR: failed to parse {template.name}: {exc!r}", file=sys.stderr)
  766:         return 4
  767: 
  768:     out_dir: Path = args.out_dir
  769:     out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  770: 
  771:     json_path = out_dir / "layout_manifest.json"
  772:     md_path = out_dir / "layout_catalog.md"
  773:     json_path.write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
  774:     md_path.write_text(render_catalog(manifest), encoding="utf-8")
  775: 
  776:     if not args.quiet:
  777:         print_summary(manifest)
  778:     print(f"wrote {json_path}")
  779:     print(f"wrote {md_path}")
  780:     return 0
  781: 
  782: 
  783: if __name__ == "__main__":
  784:     raise SystemExit(main())

 succeeded in 1233ms:
    1: # AI_Powered_PPT
    2: 
    3: AI-authored PowerPoint decks built **on the real Vieworks R&D master template**, via a
    4: schema-validated intermediate representation.
    5: 
    6: The whole design rests on one invariant:
    7: 
    8: > **The AI never emits PowerPoint.** It emits a `layout_id` plus content, as schema-valid
    9: > JSON. Every coordinate, font and colour comes from `Vieworks_R&D_ppt_Template.pptx` and
   10: > a deterministic renderer. The model writes the words; the designer still owns the design.
   11: 
   12: ```
   13:   HTML deck ─┐
   14:              ├─> [ Slide IR : JSON ] ─> [ deterministic renderer ] ─> .pptx (on template)
   15:   AI author ─┘         ▲                          │
   16:                        │                          └─> LibreOffice ─> PNG ─> visual QA
   17:                 layout_manifest.json
   18:                 (extracted from the template master)
   19: ```
   20: 
   21: Read **[`foundation_research.md`](foundation_research.md)** for the reasoning: why this
   22: shape and not the three obvious alternatives, what python-pptx cannot do, and why text
   23: overflow is the number-one risk in the entire project.
   24: 
   25: ---
   26: 
   27: ## ⚠️ Current status — read this before you run anything
   28: 
   29: **The scaffold is complete. The analyzer has never been executed.**
   30: 
   31: `src/aippt/analyze_pptx_master.py` (Deliverable C) is written and complete, but the worker
   32: session that produced this repository had **no code-execution permission** — Python was
   33: denied through Bash, through PowerShell, and through a subagent. So:
   34: 
   35: | | State |
   36: |---|---|
   37: | Repo scaffold, IR schema, prompts, stubs | ✅ complete |
   38: | `analyze_pptx_master.py` | ⚠️ **written, never run, never even syntax-checked** |
   39: | `reference/layout_manifest.json` | ❌ **placeholder — no real data** |
   40: | `reference/layout_catalog.md` | ❌ **placeholder — no real data** |
   41: | Anything about the template's internals | ❌ **unverified** |
   42: | The 30-slide HTML corpus | ✅ verified directly (see below) |
   43: 
   44: Nothing about the `.pptx` — layout count, layout names, box geometry, font sizes, or where
   45: `#DB0032` lives — is confirmed. Where `foundation_research.md` needed those facts, it says
   46: so explicitly instead of guessing. **Fabricating a layout catalog would have been the worst
   47: possible outcome**: it gets injected into every AI prompt, so the lie would propagate into
   48: every generated deck and surface far downstream, looking like a renderer bug.
   49: 
   50: ### Your first move
   51: 
   52: ```bash
   53: pip install -e .
   54: python -m aippt.analyze_pptx_master        # ~2 seconds
   55: ```
   56: 
   57: Expect to fix a bug or two — this code has never had an interpreter look at it. Then paste
   58: the stdout summary into `foundation_research.md` §4, which is holding a marked placeholder
   59: for exactly that.
   60: 
   61: ---
   62: 
   63: ## Install
   64: 
   65: ```bash
   66: pip install -e .              # core: python-pptx, lxml, Pillow
   67: pip install -e ".[html]"      # + BeautifulSoup, jsonschema  (Phase 1)
   68: pip install -e ".[ai]"        # + anthropic / openai / google-genai  (Phase 2)
   69: ```
   70: 
   71: Python ≥ 3.10 (the code uses `X | None` unions).
   72: 
   73: Pillow is **not** optional. Without it `picture.image.size` is unavailable and
   74: `add_picture()` silently stretches images instead of aspect-fitting them.
   75: 
   76: Phase 3 additionally needs system binaries that pip cannot supply: LibreOffice (`soffice`),
   77: poppler (`pdftoppm`), and Node + `@mermaid-js/mermaid-cli` (`mmdc`).
   78: 
   79: ---
   80: 
   81: ## Layout
   82: 
   83: ```
   84: README.md                     you are here
   85: foundation_research.md        the design document — start here
   86: pyproject.toml
   87: requirements.txt
   88: 
   89: src/aippt/
   90:   analyze_pptx_master.py      ✅ PoC: template -> layout_manifest.json + layout_catalog.md
   91:   ir/
   92:     schema.json               ✅ the IR contract (normative)
   93:     models.py                 ✅ typed mirror + the rules JSON Schema can't express
   94:   adapters/
   95:     html_adapter.py           📋 spec only — HTML card deck -> IR
   96:     ai_adapter.py             📋 spec only — LLM -> IR (Claude / Codex / Gemini)
   97:   render/
   98:     renderer.py               📋 spec only — IR -> .pptx on the template's layouts
   99:     fit.py                    📋 spec only — overflow control (the hard problem)
  100:     diagrams.py               📋 spec only — mermaid -> native shapes or a picture
  101: 
  102: prompts/
  103:   00_system_deck_author.md    the invariant + the content budgets
  104:   10_outline_storyboard.md    stage 1 — deck narrative
  105:   20_slide_fill.md            stage 2 — per-slide content
  106:   30_visual_qa.md             stage 3 — vision critique of rendered PNGs
  107: 
  108: reference/
  109:   layout_manifest.json        ❌ GENERATED by the analyzer — currently a placeholder
  110:   layout_catalog.md           ❌ GENERATED by the analyzer — currently a placeholder
  111: ```
  112: 
  113: `📋 spec only` means the body is `raise NotImplementedError` but the docstring is a complete
  114: contract — inputs, outputs, invariants, and the specific traps. You should be able to
  115: implement each one without reading anything else.
  116: 
  117: There are no `__init__.py` files: `aippt` is a PEP-420 namespace package. This is
  118: deliberate — the approved write scope for this task did not include them, and setuptools'
  119: `packages.find` handles namespace packages by default.
  120: 
  121: ---
  122: 
  123: ## The corpus (this part *is* verified)
  124: 
  125: Three existing HTML decks, 30 slides, sitting next to this repo. They are simultaneously
  126: the design corpus, the few-shot exemplars, and the Phase 1 test fixtures.
  127: 
  128: | Deck | Slides |
  129: |---|---|
  130: | `Slide Template.html` | 4 |
  131: | `[REDACTED].html` | 17 |
  132: | `3D Logic 개발 테스트벤치 설계안_Ver2.html` | 9 |
  133: 
  134: All three share one convention: `<section class="card" data-slide="N">` is exactly one
  135: slide, and each card holds exactly one `<h2>` (30 cards, 30 `<h2>` — verified).
  136: 
  137: Counted directly from the source, and it reshaped the IR:
  138: 
  139: | | Count | Consequence |
  140: |---|---|---|
  141: | inline `<code>` | **111** | run-level styling is mandatory, not a nicety |
  142: | `<h3>` | 28 | `heading` is a first-class block |
  143: | `<ul>` | 22 | |
  144: | `<table>` | 11 | the densest, most overflow-prone block |
  145: | `<svg>` | 7 | hand-drawn diagrams the mermaid path won't catch |
  146: | callouts | 7 | **3 variants** — `.alert-box` ×4, `.warn-box` ×2, `.ok-box` ×1 |
  147: | base64 images | 3 | all `image/jpeg` — **not** png |
  148: | `<pre>` | 2 | one of them is a tabbed C++/C# pair |
  149: 
  150: Three of these contradict the brief this repo was built from — it described a single
  151: `.alert-box` callout, PNG images, and did not mention `.tabs`, `.step-badge` (×10),
  152: `.mini-card` (×9) or the `sim-*` interactive panels at all. The IR follows the corpus, not
  153: the brief. See `foundation_research.md` §3.
  154: 
  155: ---
  156: 
  157: ## Roadmap
  158: 
  159: | Phase | Scope | Gate to pass |
  160: |---|---|---|
  161: | **0** | Run the analyzer. Get real layout facts. | `layout_manifest.json` has real data |
  162: | **1** | `html_adapter` → IR → `renderer` → `.pptx`. **No AI.** | All 30 corpus slides round-trip with a clean `ConversionReport` |
  163: | **2** | `ai_adapter`: topic + sources → IR, structured output + bounded retry | A generated deck opens in PowerPoint, on-brand, nothing overflowing |
  164: | **3** | Render → PNG → deterministic geometry checks → vision critique → repair | The loop converges without a human |
  165: 
  166: Phase 1 has no AI in it on purpose. If a deck we *already have* cannot survive the
  167: round trip, no amount of prompt engineering will save the AI path — and Phase 1 failures
  168: are debuggable in a way that "the model wrote something odd" never is.

codex
첫 대조에서 이미 핵심 불일치가 보입니다. 생성된 카탈로그의 모든 “제목” 자리도 실제로는 `TITLE`이 아니라 `BODY` placeholder이며, 다수 레이아웃은 본문 placeholder가 전혀 없습니다. 템플릿 내부 XML까지 읽기 전용으로 확인해 이것이 단순 분류 오류인지, 기존 예제 슬라이드의 자유 배치 도형을 놓친 것인지 판별하겠습니다.

   48: @dataclass
   49: class Run:
   50:     """One styled span. ``code`` is the workhorse: 111 of them in the 30-slide corpus."""
   51: 
   52:     t: str
   53:     b: bool = False
   54:     i: bool = False
   55:     code: bool = False
   56:     link: str | None = None
   57: 
   58:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
   59:         out: dict[str, Any] = {"t": self.t}
   60:         if self.b:
   61:             out["b"] = True
   62:         if self.i:
   63:             out["i"] = True
   64:         if self.code:
   65:             out["code"] = True
   66:         if self.link:
   67:             out["link"] = self.link
   68:         return out
   69: 
   70: 
   71: @dataclass
   72: class RichText:
   73:     """A run sequence. Accepts the bare-string shorthand and normalises it.
   74: 
   75:     The shorthand exists for the LLM's benefit: asking a model to emit
   76:     ``[{"t": "hello"}]`` when it means ``"hello"`` is a reliable source of malformed
   77:     output. We accept both and normalise inward, so the renderer only ever sees runs.
   78:     """
   79: 
   80:     runs: list[Run] = field(default_factory=list)
   81: 
   82:     @classmethod
   83:     def parse(cls, raw: Any, path: str) -> RichText:
   84:         if isinstance(raw, str):
   85:             return cls([Run(t=raw)])
   86:         if isinstance(raw, list):
   87:             runs = []
   88:             for i, r in enumerate(raw):
   89:                 if not isinstance(r, dict) or "t" not in r:
   90:                     raise IRValidationError(f"{path}[{i}]", "each run needs a 't' field")
   91:                 runs.append(Run(t=r["t"], b=bool(r.get("b")), i=bool(r.get("i")),
   92:                                 code=bool(r.get("code")), link=r.get("link")))
   93:             return cls(runs)
   94:         raise IRValidationError(path, f"rich text must be a string or run array, got {type(raw).__name__}")
   95: 
   96:     @property
   97:     def plain(self) -> str:
   98:         """Style-stripped text. This -- not the run count -- is what the budget measures."""
   99:         return "".join(r.t for r in self.runs)
  100: 
  101:     def to_dict(self) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
  102:         return [r.to_dict() for r in self.runs]
  103:

  342: def _parse_block(raw: Any, path: str) -> Block:
  343:     if not isinstance(raw, dict):
  344:         raise IRValidationError(path, "block must be an object")
  345:     btype = raw.get("type")
  346: 
  347:     if btype == "heading":
  348:         return Heading(text=raw["text"])
  349: 
  350:     if btype == "paragraph":
  351:         return Paragraph(text=RichText.parse(raw["text"], f"{path}.text"))
  352: 
  353:     if btype == "bullets":
  354:         items = [BulletItem(text=RichText.parse(it["text"], f"{path}.items[{i}].text"),
  355:                             level=int(it.get("level", 0)))
  356:                  for i, it in enumerate(raw.get("items", []))]
  357:         if not items:
  358:             raise IRValidationError(path, "bullets block has no items")
  359:         return Bullets(items=items)

  464: 
  465:     # -- parsing [REDACTED]
  466: 
  467:     @classmethod
  468:     def from_dict(cls, raw: dict[str, Any]) -> Deck:
  469:         """Structural parse. Does NOT check ``layout_id`` or budgets -- those need the
  470:         manifest, so they live in :meth:`validate_against_manifest` and in fit.py."""
  471:         version = raw.get("ir_version")
  472:         if version != IR_VERSION:
  473:             raise IRValidationError("ir_version",
  474:                                     f"expected {IR_VERSION!r}, got {version!r}")
  475:         d = raw.get("deck") or {}
  476:         if not d.get("title"):
  477:             raise IRValidationError("deck.title", "required")
  478:         meta = DeckMeta(title=d["title"], subtitle=d.get("subtitle"),
  479:                         language=d.get("language", "mixed"), source=d.get("source"))
  480: 
  481:         slides = []
  482:         for i, s in enumerate(raw.get("slides", [])):
  483:             path = f"slides[{i}]"
  484:             if not s.get("layout_id"):
  485:                 raise IRValidationError(f"{path}.layout_id", "required")
  486:             if not s.get("title"):
  487:                 raise IRValidationError(f"{path}.title", "required")
  488:             blocks = [_parse_block(b, f"{path}.blocks[{j}]")
  489:                       for j, b in enumerate(s.get("blocks", []))]
  490:             slides.append(Slide(layout_id=s["layout_id"], title=s["title"], blocks=blocks,
  491:                                 id=s.get("id"), notes=s.get("notes")))
  492:         if not slides:
  493:             raise IRValidationError("slides", "a deck needs at least one slide")
  494:         return cls(deck=meta, slides=slides, ir_version=version)

  519:     def validate_against_manifest(self, manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
  520:         """Cross-check every ``layout_id`` against the real template.
  521: 
  522:         Returns a list of human-readable errors (empty == valid). Returning rather than
  523:         raising is deliberate: the AI-adapter's retry prompt wants *all* the problems at
  524:         once, not just the first one.
  525:         """
  526:         known: set[str] = set()
  527:         for master in manifest.get("masters", []):
  528:             for lay in master.get("layouts", []):
  529:                 known.add(lay["layout_id"])
  530: 
  531:         errors: list[str] = []
  532:         for i, slide in enumerate(self.slides):
  533:             if slide.layout_id not in known:
  534:                 nearest = sorted(known)[:5]
  535:                 errors.append(
  536:                     f"slides[{i}].layout_id: {slide.layout_id!r} is not a layout in this "
  537:                     f"template. Valid ids include: {', '.join(repr(k) for k in nearest)}"
  538:                 )
  539:         return errors
